}{},}\frac{\mathit{x}\,\mathit{fresh}\mathbf{get}\,(\mathit{F},\mathit{use})=((\mathit{k},\iota),\mathit{F^{\prime}})\iota\in\mathit{dom}\,(\mathit{R}.\mathit{S})\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\mathit{R^{\prime}}=(\mathit{r},\mathit{S^{\prime}}\uplus\mathit{R}.\mathit{S})\mathit{F^{\prime\prime}}=\mathit{F^{\prime}},\mathit{x}\mapsto(\textsf{mut},\iota)}{\langle(\mathit{r},\mathit{S},\mathit{F})\mathop{::}\mathit{RS};(\mathit{r},\mathit{S^{\prime}})*H_{\mathit{op}};\mathit{R}*H_{\mathit{cl}};H_{\mathit{fr}}\rangle\,\xrightarrow{\textit{merge}(\mathit{x},\mathit{use})}\,\langle(\mathit{r},\mathit{S},\mathit{F^{\prime\prime}})\mathop{::}\mathit{RS};\mathit{R^{\prime}}*H_{\mathit{op}};H_{\mathit{cl}};H_{\mathit{fr}}\rangle}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={\hypertarget{ottalt:rule:ott:region-merge}{{{region-merge}}}
}{},}\frac{\mathit{x}\,\mathit{fresh}\mathbf{get}\,(\mathit{F},\mathit{use})=((\mathit{k},\iota),\mathit{F^{\prime}})\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\mathsf{cfg\_load}((\mathit{r},\mathit{S},\mathit{F^{\prime}})\mathop{::}\mathit{RS},H_{\mathit{op}}*H_{\mathit{cl}}*H_{\mathit{fr}},\iota)=(\#\mathit{C},\,\mathbf{\_}\,)\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\mathit{F^{\prime\prime}}=\mathit{F^{\prime}},\mathit{x}\mapsto(\mathit{k},\iota)}{\langle(\mathit{r},\mathit{S},\mathit{F})\mathop{::}\mathit{RS};H_{\mathit{op}};H_{\mathit{cl}};H_{\mathit{fr}}\rangle\,\xrightarrow{\textit{cast}(\mathit{x},\mathit{use},\mathit{k}~C)}\,\langle(\mathit{r},\mathit{S},\mathit{F^{\prime\prime}})\mathop{::}\mathit{RS};H_{\mathit{op}};H_{\mathit{cl}};H_{\mathit{fr}}\rangle}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={\hypertarget{ottalt:rule:ott:region-cast}{{{region-cast}}}
}{},}\frac{\mathit{x}\,\mathit{fresh}\mathbf{get}\,(\mathit{F},\mathit{use})=((\mathit{k},\iota),\mathit{F^{\prime}})\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\mathsf{cfg\_load}((\mathit{r},\mathit{S},\mathit{F^{\prime}})\mathop{::}\mathit{RS},H_{\mathit{op}}*H_{\mathit{cl}}*H_{\mathit{fr}},\iota)=(\#\mathit{C},\,\mathbf{\_}\,)\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\mathit{F^{\prime\prime}}=\mathit{F^{\prime}},\mathit{x}\mapsto(\mathit{k},\iota)}{\langle(\mathit{r},\mathit{S},\mathit{F})\mathop{::}\mathit{RS};H_{\mathit{op}};H_{\mathit{cl}};H_{\mathit{fr}}\rangle\,\xrightarrow{\textit{nocast}(\mathit{x},\mathit{use},\mathit{k}~C)}\,\langle(\mathit{r},\mathit{S},\mathit{F^{\prime\prime}})\mathop{::}\mathit{RS};H_{\mathit{op}};H_{\mathit{cl}};H_{\mathit{fr}}\rangle}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={\hypertarget{ottalt:rule:ott:region-nocast}{{{region-nocast}}}
}{},}\frac{\mathit{w}\,\mathit{fresh}\forall\mathit{i}\in[1,\mathit{n}].~\mathit{z_{\mathit{i}}}\,\mathit{fresh}\forall\mathit{i}\in[1,\mathit{n}].~\mathbf{get}\,(\mathit{F_{\mathit{i}}},\mathit{use_{\mathit{i}}})=((\mathit{k_{\mathit{i}}},\iota_{\mathit{i}}),F_{i+1})\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\forall\mathit{i}\in[1,\mathit{n}].~\mathit{v^{\prime}_{\mathit{i}}}=\begin{cases}(\mathit{k_{\mathit{i}}},\iota_{\mathit{i}})&\text{if }\mathit{k_{\mathit{i}}}=\textsf{iso}\hskip 9.24994pt(\textsf{paused}\mathop{\odot}\mathit{k_{\mathit{i}}},\iota_{\mathit{i}})&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\mathit{F}=[\mathit{z_{\mathit{i}}}\mapsto\mathit{v^{\prime}_{\mathit{i}}}~|~\mathit{i}\in[1,\mathit{n}]]F_{n+1}(\mathit{y})=(\,\mathbf{\_}\,,\iota)\mathsf{cfg\_load}((\mathit{r},\mathit{S},\mathit{F_{{\mathrm{1}}}})\mathop{::}\mathit{RS},H_{\mathit{op}},\iota)=\mathit{o}[\mathit{f}\mapsto(\,\mathbf{\_}\,,\iota^{\prime})]\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\iota^{\prime}\in\mathit{dom}\,(\mathit{S^{\prime}})\iota^{\prime\prime}\,\mathit{fresh}\mathit{F^{\prime}}=\mathit{F},\mathit{w}\mapsto(\mathit{k},\iota^{\prime\prime})\mathit{RF}=(\mathit{r^{\prime}},[\iota^{\prime\prime}\mapsto(\texttt{\#Cell},[\texttt{val}\mapsto(\textsf{mut},\iota^{\prime})])],\mathit{F^{\prime}})}{\langle(\mathit{r},\mathit{S},\mathit{F_{{\mathrm{1}}}})\mathop{::}\mathit{RS};H_{\mathit{op}};(\mathit{r^{\prime}},\mathit{S^{\prime}})*H_{\mathit{cl}};H_{\mathit{fr}}\rangle\,\xrightarrow{\mathit{enter}\,(\mathit{w},\mathit{k},\mathit{y}.\mathit{f},\mathit{z_{{\mathrm{1}}}}=\mathit{use_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\,...\,,\mathit{z_{\mathit{n}}}=\mathit{use_{\mathit{n}}})}\,\langle\mathit{RF}\mathop{::}(\mathit{r},\mathit{S},F_{n+1})\mathop{::}\mathit{RS};(\mathit{r^{\prime}},\mathit{S^{\prime}})*H_{\mathit{op}};H_{\mathit{cl}};H_{\mathit{fr}}\rangle}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={\hypertarget{ottalt:rule:ott:region-enter-ok}{{{region-enter-ok}}}
}{},}\frac{\forall\mathit{i}\in[1,\mathit{n}].~\mathbf{get}\,(\mathit{F_{\mathit{i}}},\mathit{use_{\mathit{i}}})=((\mathit{k_{\mathit{i}}},\iota_{\mathit{i}}),F_{i+1})F_{n+1}(\mathit{y})=(\,\mathbf{\_}\,,\iota)\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\mathsf{cfg\_load}((\mathit{r},\mathit{S},\mathit{F})\mathop{::}\mathit{RS},H_{\mathit{op}},\iota)=\mathit{o}[\mathit{f}\mapsto(\,\mathbf{\_}\,,\iota^{\prime})]\forall\mathit{R^{\prime}}\in H_{\mathit{cl}}.~\iota^{\prime}\,\not\in\,\mathit{dom}\,(\mathit{R^{\prime}}.\mathit{S})}{\langle(\mathit{r},\mathit{S},\mathit{F})\mathop{::}\mathit{RS};H_{\mathit{op}};H_{\mathit{cl}};H_{\mathit{fr}}\rangle\,\xrightarrow{\textit{badenter}(\mathit{y}.\mathit{f})}\,\langle(\mathit{r},\mathit{S},\mathit{F})\mathop{::}\mathit{RS};H_{\mathit{op}};H_{\mathit{cl}};H_{\mathit{fr}}\rangle}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={\hypertarget{ottalt:rule:ott:region-enter-fail}{{{region-enter-fail}}}
}{},}\frac{\mathit{x}\,\mathit{fresh}\mathbf{get}\,(\mathit{F^{\prime}},\mathit{use})=(\mathit{v},\mathit{F^{\prime\prime}})\mathit{F^{\prime\prime}}(\mathit{z})=(\,\mathbf{\_}\,,\iota^{\prime})\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\mathsf{load}(\mathit{S^{\prime}},\iota^{\prime})=\mathit{o^{\prime}}[\mathit{f^{\prime}}\mapsto(\,\mathbf{\_}\,,\iota^{\prime\prime})]\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\mathit{F}(\mathit{y})=(\,\mathbf{\_}\,,\iota)\mathsf{stack\_load}((\mathit{r},\mathit{S},\mathit{F})\mathop{::}\mathit{RS},\iota)=\mathit{o}[\mathit{f}\mapsto(\mathit{k},\,\mathbf{\_}\,)]\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\mathsf{stack\_store}((\mathit{r},\mathit{S},\mathit{F})\mathop{::}\mathit{RS},\iota,\mathit{o}[\mathit{f}\mapsto(\mathit{k},\iota^{\prime\prime})])=(\mathit{r},\mathit{S^{\prime\prime}},\mathit{F})\mathop{::}\mathit{RS^{\prime}}\mathit{F^{\prime\prime\prime}}=\mathit{F},\mathit{x}\mapsto\mathit{v}}{\langle(\mathit{r^{\prime}},\mathit{S^{\prime}},\mathit{F^{\prime}})\mathop{::}(\mathit{r},\mathit{S},\mathit{F})\mathop{::}\mathit{RS};(\mathit{r},\mathit{S}_{\mathit{op}})*H_{\mathit{op}};H_{\mathit{cl}};H_{\mathit{fr}}\rangle\,\xrightarrow{\mathit{exit}\,(\mathit{x},\mathit{use},\mathit{y}.\mathit{f},\mathit{z}.\mathit{f^{\prime}})}\,\langle(\mathit{r},\mathit{S^{\prime\prime}},\mathit{F^{\prime\prime\prime}})\mathop{::}\mathit{RS^{\prime}};H_{\mathit{op}};(\mathit{r},\mathit{S}_{\mathit{op}})*H_{\mathit{cl}};H_{\mathit{fr}}\rangle}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={\hypertarget{ottalt:rule:ott:region-exit-temp}{{{region-exit-temp}}}
}{},}\frac{\mathit{x}\,\mathit{fresh}\mathbf{get}\,(\mathit{F^{\prime}},\mathit{use})=(\mathit{v},\mathit{F^{\prime\prime}})\mathit{F^{\prime\prime}}(\mathit{z})=(\,\mathbf{\_}\,,\iota^{\prime})\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\mathsf{load}(\mathit{S^{\prime}},\iota^{\prime})=\mathit{o^{\prime}}[\mathit{f^{\prime}}\mapsto(\,\mathbf{\_}\,,\iota^{\prime\prime})]\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\mathit{F}(\mathit{y})=(\,\mathbf{\_}\,,\iota)\mathsf{heap\_load}(H_{\mathit{op}},\iota)=\mathit{o}[\mathit{f}\mapsto(\mathit{k},\,\mathbf{\_}\,)]\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\mathsf{heap\_store}(H_{\mathit{op}},\iota,\mathit{o}[\mathit{f}\mapsto(\mathit{k},\iota^{\prime\prime})])=H_{\mathit{op}}^{\prime}\mathit{F^{\prime\prime\prime}}=\mathit{F},\mathit{x}\mapsto\mathit{v}}{\langle(\mathit{r^{\prime}},\mathit{S^{\prime}},\mathit{F^{\prime}})\mathop{::}(\mathit{r},\mathit{S},\mathit{F})\mathop{::}\mathit{RS};(\mathit{r^{\prime}},\mathit{S}_{\mathit{op}})*H_{\mathit{op}};H_{\mathit{cl}};H_{\mathit{fr}}\rangle\,\xrightarrow{\mathit{exit}\,(\mathit{x},\mathit{use},\mathit{y}.\mathit{f},\mathit{z}.\mathit{f^{\prime}})}\,\langle(\mathit{r},\mathit{S},\mathit{F^{\prime\prime\prime}})\mathop{::}\mathit{RS};H_{\mathit{op}}^{\prime};(\mathit{r^{\prime}},\mathit{S}_{\mathit{op}})*H_{\mathit{cl}};H_{\mathit{fr}}\rangle}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={\hypertarget{ottalt:rule:ott:region-exit-heap}{{{region-exit-heap}}}
}{},}\frac{\forall\mathit{i}\in[1,\mathit{n}].~\mathit{x_{\mathit{i}}}\,\mathit{fresh}\forall\mathit{i}\in[1,\mathit{n}].~\mathbf{get}\,(\mathit{F_{\mathit{i}}},\mathit{use_{\mathit{i}}})=(\mathit{v_{\mathit{i}}},F_{i+1})\mathit{F^{\prime}}=F_{n+1}+[\mathit{x_{\mathit{i}}}\mapsto\mathit{v_{\mathit{i}}}~|~\mathit{i}\in[1,\mathit{n}]]}{\langle(\mathit{r},\mathit{S},\mathit{F_{{\mathrm{1}}}})\mathop{::}\mathit{RS};H_{\mathit{op}};H_{\mathit{cl}};H_{\mathit{fr}}\rangle\,\xrightarrow{\textit{bind}(\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}=\mathit{use_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\,...\,,\mathit{x_{\mathit{n}}}=\mathit{use_{\mathit{n}}})}\,\langle(\mathit{r},\mathit{S},\mathit{F^{\prime}})\mathop{::}\mathit{RS};H_{\mathit{op}};H_{\mathit{cl}};H_{\mathit{fr}}\rangle}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={\hypertarget{ottalt:rule:ott:region-bind}{{{region-bind}}}
}{},}\frac{}{\mathit{rcfg}\,\xrightarrow{\epsilon}\,\mathit{rcfg}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={\hypertarget{ottalt:rule:ott:region-eps}{{{region-eps}}}
}{},}

C.1.1.
Helper Functions and Predicates

We use the following helper definitions:

Viewpoint adaptation of a value amounts to viewpoint adaptation on
its capability (defined in Section
E.2
).

k\mathop{\odot}(k^{\prime}~\iota)=(k\mathop{\odot}k^{\prime})~\iota

We can look up objects in a store
S
given their identifier.

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\mathsf{load}((\iota\mapsto o,S),\iota^{\prime})&=&\begin{cases}o&\text{if }\iota=\iota^{\prime}\\
\mathsf{load}(S,\iota^{\prime})&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}\\
\mathsf{load}(\varepsilon,\iota^{\prime})&=&~\downarrow\end{array}

Looking up an object on the region stack goes through the local
stores of each frame from the top down.

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\mathsf{stack\_load}((r,S,F)::RS,\iota)&=&\begin{cases}o&\text{if }\mathsf{load}(S,\iota)=o\\
\mathsf{stack\_load}(S,\iota)&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}\\
\mathsf{stack\_load}(\varepsilon,\iota)&=&~\downarrow\end{array}

Looking up an object on the heap goes through the regions left to
right.

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\mathsf{heap\_load}(H_{1}*H_{2},\iota)&=&\begin{cases}o&\text{if }\mathsf{heap\_load}(H_{1},\iota)=o\\
\mathsf{heap\_load}(H_{2},\iota)&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}\\
\mathsf{heap\_load}((r,S),\iota)&=&\mathsf{load}(S,\iota)p\end{array}

The top-level lookup function
\mathsf{cfg\_load}
performs a
lookup on a given stack and heap using the helper functions above.
Note that an object identifier
\iota
can only appear in the
domain of a single store
S
in a well-formed configuration.

\mathsf{cfg\_load}(RS,H,\iota)=\begin{cases}o&\text{if }\mathsf{stack\_load}(RS,\iota)=o\\
\mathsf{heap\_load}(H,\iota)&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

Similarly, we can update a store
S
with a new object for a given
object identifier.

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\mathsf{store}((\iota\mapsto o,S),\iota^{\prime},o^{\prime})&=&\begin{cases}\iota\mapsto o^{\prime},S&\text{if }\iota=\iota^{\prime}\\
\iota\mapsto o,\mathsf{store}(S,\iota^{\prime},o^{\prime})&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}\\
\mathsf{store}(\varepsilon,\iota^{\prime},o^{\prime})&=&~\downarrow\end{array}

When storing an object on the stack, we always store in the
top-most frame, so we don’t need a function for traversing the
stack. When storing to the heap, we go from left to right.

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\mathsf{heap\_store}(H_{1}*H_{2},\iota,o)&=&\begin{cases}H_{1}^{\prime}*H_{2}&\text{if }\mathsf{heap\_store}(H_{1},\iota,o)=H_{1}^{\prime}\\
H_{1}*\mathsf{heap\_store}(H_{2},\iota,o)&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}\\
\mathsf{heap\_store}((r,S),\iota,o)&=&\begin{cases}(r,S^{\prime})&\text{if }\mathsf{store}(S,\iota,o)=S^{\prime}\\
~\downarrow~&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}\end{array}

When freezing a region, we need to calculate the reachable regions
from the region being frozen. We define a relation

\mathsf{reachable\_step}
which holds when a region
R
has an
object with a reference to an object in another region
R^{\prime}
.
Calculating the reachable regions amounts to calculating the
transitive closure of
\mathsf{reachable\_step}
.

\begin{array}[]{l}\mathsf{reachable\_regions}(R,H)=\{R^{\prime}~|~\mathsf{reachable\_step}^{+}(R,R^{\prime})\}\\
\quad\textit{where}\\
\quad\mathsf{reachable\_step}(R,R^{\prime})\equiv R^{\prime}\in H\land o[f\mapsto\iota]\in\mathbf{rng}(R.S)\land\iota\in\mathbf{dom}(R^{\prime}.S)\par\end{array}

C.2.
Well-Formedness Rules

\overline{\Gamma}\vdash\mathit{rcfg}

(Region configuration)

\frac{\exists\Delta,\Psi.~\overline{\Gamma};\Delta;\Psi\vdash\mathit{rcfg}\quad\quad\mathsf{capability\_ok}\,(\overline{\Gamma},\mathit{rcfg})\quad\quad\mathsf{topology\_ok}\,(\overline{\Gamma},\mathit{rcfg})}{\overline{\Gamma}\vdash\mathit{rcfg}}\quad\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={\hypertarget{ottalt:rule:ott:wf-rcfg}{{{wf-rcfg}}}
}{},}

\overline{\Gamma};\Delta;\Psi\vdash\mathit{rcfg}

(Region configuration types)

\frac{\Delta_{RS}\uplus\Delta_{op}\uplus\Delta_{cl}\uplus\Delta_{fr}=\Delta\Psi_{op}\uplus\Psi_{cl}\uplus\Psi_{fr}=\Psi\overline{\Gamma};\Delta;\Delta_{RS};\Psi_{op}\vdash\mathit{RS}\quad\quad\Delta;\Delta_{op};\Psi_{op}\vdash H_{\mathit{op}}\Delta;\Delta_{cl};\Psi_{cl}\vdash H_{\mathit{cl}}\Delta;\Delta_{fr};\Psi_{fr}\vdash H_{\mathit{fr}}}{\overline{\Gamma};\Delta;\Psi\vdash\langle\mathit{RS};H_{\mathit{op}};H_{\mathit{cl}};H_{\mathit{fr}}\rangle}\quad\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={\hypertarget{ottalt:rule:ott:wf-ty-rcfg}{{{wf-ty-rcfg}}}
}{},}

\overline{\Gamma};\Delta;\Delta;\Psi\vdash\mathit{RS}

(Region stack types)

\frac{\Delta^{\prime}=\Delta_{{\mathrm{1}}}\uplus\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}}\Delta;\Delta_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash\mathit{S}\quad\quad\Delta;\Gamma\vdash\mathit{F}\overline{\Gamma};\Delta;\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}};\Psi\vdash\mathit{RS}}{\Gamma::\overline{\Gamma};\Delta;\Delta^{\prime};\mathit{r},\Psi\vdash(\mathit{r},\mathit{S},\mathit{F})\mathop{::}\mathit{RS}}\quad\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={\hypertarget{ottalt:rule:ott:wf-rs-cons}{{{wf-rs-cons}}}
}{},}\frac{\vdash\Delta}{\varepsilon_{\overline{\Gamma}};\Delta;\varepsilon_{\Delta};\varepsilon_{\Psi}\vdash\varepsilon_{RS}}\quad\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={\hypertarget{ottalt:rule:ott:wf-rs-nil}{{{wf-rs-nil}}}
}{},}

\Delta;\Delta;\Psi\vdash H

(Region heap types)

\frac{\Delta_{{\mathrm{1}}}\uplus\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}}=\Delta^{\prime}\Psi_{{\mathrm{1}}}\uplus\Psi_{{\mathrm{2}}}=\Psi\quad\quad\Delta;\Delta_{{\mathrm{1}}};\Psi_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash H_{{\mathrm{1}}}\Delta;\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}};\Psi_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash H_{{\mathrm{2}}}}{\Delta;\Delta^{\prime};\Psi\vdash H_{{\mathrm{1}}}*H_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\quad\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={\hypertarget{ottalt:rule:ott:wf-heap-prod}{{{wf-heap-prod}}}
}{},}\frac{\Delta;\Delta^{\prime}\vdash\mathit{S}}{\Delta;\Delta^{\prime};\mathit{r}\vdash(\mathit{r},\mathit{S})}\quad\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={\hypertarget{ottalt:rule:ott:wf-heap-sing}{{{wf-heap-sing}}}
}{},}

\Delta;\Delta\vdash S

(Region subheap types)

\frac{\iota:\mathit{CL}\in\Delta\#CL\mathop{\text{<\#:}}\mathit{CL}\Delta;\mathbf{ftypes}\,(\mathit{CL})\vdash\mathit{F}\Delta;\Delta^{\prime}\vdash\mathit{S}}{\Delta;\Delta^{\prime},\iota:\mathit{CL}\vdash\mathit{S},\iota\mapsto(\#CL,\mathit{F})}\quad\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={\hypertarget{ottalt:rule:ott:wf-subheap-cons}{{{wf-subheap-cons}}}
}{},}\frac{}{\Delta;\varepsilon_{\Delta}\vdash\varepsilon_{S}}\quad\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={\hypertarget{ottalt:rule:ott:wf-subheap-nil}{{{wf-subheap-nil}}}
}{},}

\Delta;\Gamma\vdash F

(Region variable types)

\frac{\iota:\mathit{CL}\in\Delta\mathit{k}~\mathit{CL}<:\mathit{t}\mathit{x}\,\not\in\,\mathit{dom}\,(\mathit{F})}{\Delta;\Gamma,\mathit{x}:\mathit{t}\vdash\mathit{F},\mathit{x}\mapsto(\mathit{k},\iota)}\quad\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={\hypertarget{ottalt:rule:ott:wf-vars-cons}{{{wf-vars-cons}}}
}{},}\frac{}{\Delta;\varepsilon\vdash\varepsilon_{F}}\quad\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={\hypertarget{ottalt:rule:ott:wf-vars-nil}{{{wf-vars-nil}}}
}{},}

\mathsf{\#}\mathit{CL}\mathop{\text{<\#:}}~\mathit{CL}

(Subtagging)

\frac{}{\#\mathit{C}\mathop{\text{<\#:}}C}\quad\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={\hypertarget{ottalt:rule:ott:subtag-class}{{{subtag-class}}}
}{},}\frac{}{\texttt{\#Cell}\mathop{\text{<\#:}}\texttt{Cell}[\mathit{t}]}\quad\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={\hypertarget{ottalt:rule:ott:subtag-cell}{{{subtag-cell}}}
}{},}

C.3.
Predicates on Graphs

We will now define some predicates used to define the invariants of our system.
These are defined in terms of the object graph of the configuration. Therefore
we first define the set of graphs
\mathit{GRAPH}
, and a mapping
G:\mathit{RCFG}\to\mathit{GRAPH}
, where
\mathit{RCFG}
is the set of region configurations.

C.3.1.
Region Configuration Graph

The the elements of
\mathit{GRAPH}
are tuples
(\mathcal{L},\mathcal{R})
of vertices
\mathcal{L}

(locations) and edges
\mathcal{R}
(refereces). We represent the vertices
and edges as

\begin{array}[]{rcll}\mathit{loc}&::=&\mathtt{heap}(r,\iota)~|~\mathtt{temp}(r,\iota)~|~\mathtt{root}(r)&\text{Location}\\
\mathit{ref}&::=&\mathit{loc}\xrightarrow{f,k}\mathit{loc}^{\prime}&\text{Reference, marked with field and
capability}\end{array}

We call the set of all locations and references
\mathit{LOCS}
and

\mathit{REFS}
respectively.

\displaystyle\mathit{rid}(\mathtt{heap}(r,\iota))

\displaystyle=\quad r

\displaystyle\mathit{rid}(\mathtt{temp}(r,\iota))

\displaystyle=\quad r

\displaystyle\mathit{rid}(\mathtt{root}(r))

\displaystyle=\quad r

Since all
\mathit{loc}
contains a region id
r
, we can define a shorthand
\mathit{loc}[\cdot]
with a hole for
r
as

\mathit{loc}[\cdot]::=\mathtt{heap}(\cdot,\iota)~|~\mathtt{temp}(\cdot,\iota)~|~\mathtt{root}(\cdot)

We define
\uplus
as the union of two disjoint sets.

A\uplus B=\begin{cases}A\cup B&\text{ if }A\cap B=\emptyset\\
~\downarrow~&\text{ otherwise}\end{cases}

where
~\downarrow~
denotes the
undefined
value. In particular,
if any of
A
or
B
is
~\downarrow~
we let
A\cap B=~\downarrow~
, and thus
A\uplus B=~\downarrow~
. For sets
S,S^{\prime}
, and
A,B\subseteq S
,
f:S\to S^{\prime}
,
we define
\uplus_{f}
by

A\uplus_{f}B=\begin{cases}A\cup B&\text{ if }I_{f}(A)\cap I_{f}(B)=\emptyset\\
~\downarrow~&\text{ otherwise}\end{cases}

where
I_{f}(A)
is the image of
f
restricted to the set
A
. Furthermore

I_{f}(~\downarrow~)=~\downarrow~
. We call
f
above a
separating function
.

For the definition of
G
we define the separating function
\mathit{seploc}
as

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathit{seploc}(\mathtt{heap}(r,\iota))&=&\mathtt{obj}(\iota)\\
\mathit{seploc}(\mathtt{temp}(r,\iota))&=&\mathtt{obj}(\iota)\\
\mathit{seploc}(\mathtt{root}(r))&=&\mathtt{root}(r)\end{array}

Assuming
\mathcal{L}^{*}=\mathcal{L}\uplus_{\mathit{seploc}}\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\neq~\downarrow~
we see that
all object ids in
\mathcal{L}^{*}
are unique, and furthermore that there is at most
one
\mathtt{root}(r)
for each region id
r
.

We are now ready to start defining the graph of a region configuration
\mathit{rcfg}
.
We write

G(\langle RS;H_{op};H_{cl};H_{fr}\rangle)=(\mathcal{L},\mathcal{R})

where

\mathcal{L}=\mathit{loc}_{RS}(RS)\uplus_{\mathit{seploc}}\mathit{loc}_{H}(H_{op})\uplus_{\mathit{seploc}}\mathit{loc}_{H}(H_{cl})\uplus_{\mathit{seploc}}\mathit{loc}_{H}(H_{fr})

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathit{loc}_{RS}((r,S,F)::RS)&=&\mathit{loc}_{S}^{\mathtt{temp}}(S)\uplus_{\mathit{seploc}}\{\mathtt{root}(r)\}\uplus_{\mathit{seploc}}\mathit{loc}_{RS}(RS)\\
\mathit{loc}_{RS}(\varepsilon)&=&\emptyset\\
\mathit{loc}_{H}(H_{1}*H_{2})&=&\mathit{loc}_{H}(H_{1})\uplus_{\mathit{seploc}}\mathit{loc}_{H}(H_{2})\\
\mathit{loc}_{H}((r,S))&=&\mathit{loc}_{S}^{\mathtt{heap}}(r;S)\\
\mathit{loc}_{S}^{\mathtt{heap}}(r;S,\iota\mapsto\_)&=&\mathit{loc}_{S}^{\mathtt{heap}}(r;S)\uplus_{\mathit{seploc}}\{\mathtt{heap}(r,\iota)\}\\
\mathit{loc}_{S}^{\mathtt{temp}}(r;S,\iota\mapsto\_)&=&\mathit{loc}_{S}^{\mathtt{temp}}(r;S)\uplus_{\mathit{seploc}}\{\mathtt{temp}(r,\iota)\}\\
\mathit{loc}_{S}^{*}(\varepsilon)&=&\emptyset\end{array}

Because of the properties of
\mathit{seploc}
mentioned above (in particular the
uniqueness of object ids
\iota
), given that
\mathcal{L}\neq~\downarrow~
we can
define a partial function
\mathit{loc}_{\mathcal{L}}:\mathit{Oid}\rightharpoonup\mathcal{L}
as

\mathit{loc}_{\mathcal{L}}(\iota)=\begin{cases}\mathtt{heap}(r,\iota)&\text{ if }\mathtt{heap}(r,\iota)\in\mathcal{L}\\
\mathtt{temp}(r,\iota)&\text{ if }\mathtt{heap}(r,\iota)\in\mathcal{L}\end{cases}

We move on to
\mathcal{R}
. We define the separating function
\mathit{sepref}
as

\mathit{sepref}\left(\mathit{loc}\xrightarrow{f,k}\mathit{loc}^{\prime}\right)=(loc,f)

We can use this as a separating function to ensure that uniqueness constraints
on field names in a well-formed configuration will be mirrored in the
construction of
\mathcal{R}
. I.e. only configurations where each object or frame
has at most one field with a certain name will have a well-defined graph.

Given
\mathit{loc}_{\mathcal{L}}
as above we have

\mathcal{R}=\mathit{ref}_{RS}(RS)\uplus^{\mathit{sepref}}\mathit{ref}_{H}(H_{op})\uplus^{\mathit{sepref}}\mathit{ref}_{H}(H_{cl})\uplus^{\mathit{sepref}}\mathit{ref}_{H}(H_{fr})

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathit{ref}_{RS}((r,S,F)::RS)&=&\mathit{ref}_{S}(S)\uplus^{\mathit{sepref}}\mathit{ref}_{F}(\mathtt{root}(r);F)\uplus^{\mathit{sepref}}\mathit{ref}_{RS}(RS)\\
\mathit{ref}_{RS}(\varepsilon)&=&\emptyset\\
\mathit{ref}_{H}(H_{1}*H_{2})&=&\mathit{ref}_{H}(H_{1})\uplus^{\mathit{sepref}}\mathit{ref}_{H}(H_{2})\\
\mathit{ref}_{H}((r,S))&=&\mathit{ref}_{S}(S)\\
\mathit{ref}_{S}(S,\iota\mapsto(CL,F))&=&\mathit{ref}_{S}(S)\uplus^{\mathit{sepref}}\mathit{ref}_{F}(\mathit{loc}_{\mathcal{L}}(\iota);F)\\
\mathit{ref}_{S}(\varepsilon)&=&\emptyset\\
\mathit{ref}_{F}(\mathit{loc};F,f\mapsto(k,\iota))&=&\mathit{ref}_{F}(F)\uplus^{\mathit{sepref}}\{\mathit{loc}\xrightarrow{f,k}\mathit{loc}_{\mathcal{L}}(\iota)\}\\
\mathit{ref}_{F}(\mathit{loc};F,f\mapsto\mathbf{undef})&=&\mathit{ref}_{F}(F)\\
\mathit{ref}_{F}(\varepsilon)&=&\emptyset\end{array}

We want to have some kind of sanity check on a graph. Therefore we define
well-formedness of a graph
\mathcal{G}
,
\mathit{WFG}(\mathcal{G})

as a predicate on a graph, as

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathit{WFG}((\mathcal{L},\mathcal{R}))&\iff&\mathit{WFSrcDst}(\mathcal{L},\mathcal{R})~\land~\mathit{WFRefs}(\mathcal{R})\\
\mathit{WFSrcDst}(\mathcal{L},\mathcal{R})&\iff&\begin{aligned} \forall&\mathit{loc}\xrightarrow{f,k}\mathit{loc}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{R}.\\
&\mathit{loc}\in\mathcal{L}~\land~\mathit{loc}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}\end{aligned}\\
\mathit{WFRefs}(\mathcal{R})&\iff&\begin{aligned} \forall&\mathit{ref}_{1}=(\mathit{loc}\xrightarrow{f,\_}\_)~,\mathit{ref}_{2}=(\mathit{loc}\xrightarrow{f^{\prime},\_}\_)\in\mathcal{R}.\\
&\mathit{ref}_{1}=\mathit{ref}_{2}~\lor~f\neq f^{\prime}\end{aligned}\end{array}

For a well-formed graph
\mathcal{G}=(\mathcal{L},\mathcal{R})
we can define the partial
function

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathit{ref}_{\mathcal{R}}(\mathit{loc},f)&=&\begin{cases}\mathit{loc}\xrightarrow{f,k}\mathit{loc}^{\prime}&\text{ if }\mathit{loc}\xrightarrow{f,k}\mathit{loc}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{R}\\
~\downarrow~&\text{ otherwise}\end{cases}\\
\end{array}

Furthermore we define shorthands

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathit{loc}_{\mathcal{G}}(\iota)&=&\mathit{loc}_{\mathcal{L}}(\iota)\\
\mathit{ref}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathit{loc},f)&=&\mathit{ref}_{\mathcal{R}}(\mathit{loc},f)\\
\mathit{ref}_{\mathcal{G}}(\iota,f)&=&\mathit{ref}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathit{loc}_{\mathcal{G}}(\iota),f)\end{array}

C.3.2.
Region Orders and Sets

We define
\rho
to talk about ordering of regions.

\begin{array}[]{rccl}\rho&::=&r\underset{x.f}{::}\rho~|~\varepsilon&\text{Region ordering}\end{array}

If we do not care about
x.f
we write a shorthand
r::\rho
.

From a well formed region stack
\overline{\Gamma}\vdash\mathit{RS}
, we can generate a
\rho
:

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\rho(\Gamma\underset{x.f}{::}\overline{\Gamma},(r,S,F)::RS)&=&r\underset{x.f}{::}\rho(RS)\\
\rho(\varepsilon,\varepsilon)&=&\varepsilon\end{array}

We extend this definition to region configurations,
\rho(\overline{\Gamma},\mathit{rcfg})
, in the
obvious way.

We define ordering of region ids under
\rho
as

\rho\vdash r<r^{\prime}\quad\rho\vdash r\leq r^{\prime}

(Region ordering)

\frac{\mathit{r}\in\rho}{\mathit{r^{\prime}}\underset{\mathit{x}.\mathit{f}}{::}\rho\vdash\mathit{r}\,<\,\mathit{r^{\prime}}}\quad\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={\hypertarget{ottalt:rule:ott:region-order-lt-cons}{{{region-order-lt-cons}}}
}{},}\frac{\rho\vdash\mathit{r}\,<\,\mathit{r^{\prime}}}{\rho\vdash\mathit{r}\,\leq\,\mathit{r^{\prime}}}\quad\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={\hypertarget{ottalt:rule:ott:region-order-leq-strict}{{{region-order-leq-strict}}}
}{},}\frac{}{\rho\vdash\mathit{r}\,\leq\,\mathit{r}}\quad\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={\hypertarget{ottalt:rule:ott:region-order-leq-eq}{{{region-order-leq-eq}}}
}{},}

We define

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathit{Regions}(H_{1}*H_{2})&=&\mathit{Regions}(H_{1})\uplus\mathit{Regions}(H_{2})\\
\mathit{Regions}((r,S))&=&\{r\}\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathit{Open}(\langle RS;H_{op};H_{cl};H_{fr}\rangle)&=&\mathit{Regions}(H_{op})\\
\mathit{Closed}(\langle RS;H_{op};H_{cl};H_{fr}\rangle)&=&\mathit{Regions}(H_{cl})\\
\mathit{Frozen}(\langle RS;H_{op};H_{cl};H_{fr}\rangle)&=&\mathit{Regions}(H_{fr})\end{array}

We define intersection of
\rho
and a set of region ids in the obvious way. The
relation
\vdash\rho,\mathit{Cl},\mathit{Fr}
is simply disjointness of
\rho
and the region id
sets
\mathit{Cl},\mathit{Fr}
:

\vdash\rho,\mathit{Cl},\mathit{Fr}\iff\rho\cap\mathit{Cl}=\rho\cap\mathit{Fr}=\mathit{Cl}\cap\mathit{Fr}=\emptyset

C.3.3.
Capability Semantics

The predicate
\mathsf{capability\_ok}
(
\mathit{rcfg}
) is defined using three auxilliary
predicates as follows.

\displaystyle\mathsf{capability\_ok}(\overline{\Gamma},\mathit{rcfg})\iff

\displaystyle\mathsf{var\_unique}(G(\mathit{rcfg}))~\land~

\displaystyle\mathsf{region\_order}(\rho(\overline{\Gamma},\mathit{rcfg}),\mathit{Closed}(\mathit{rcfg}),\mathit{Frozen}(\mathit{rcfg}),G(\mathit{rcfg}))~\land~

\displaystyle\mathsf{location\_ok}(G(\mathit{rcfg}))

\displaystyle\mathsf{deep\_freeze}(\mathit{Frozen}(\mathit{rcfg}),G(\mathit{rcfg}))

The three auxilliary predicates are defined using the object graph
G(\mathit{rcfg})
as
follows.

\mathsf{region\_order}(\rho,\mathit{Cl},\mathit{Fr},\mathcal{G})\iff\forall\mathit{ref}\in\mathcal{G}.\mathsf{region\_order}1(\rho,\mathit{Cl},\mathit{Fr},\mathit{ref})

\displaystyle\mathsf{region\_order}1(\rho,\mathit{Cl},\mathit{Fr},

\displaystyle(\mathit{loc}[r]\xrightarrow{f,k}\mathit{loc}^{\prime}[r^{\prime}]))

\displaystyle\iff

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &(k=\textsf{mut}&\implies&r=r^{\prime})&~\land~\\
&(k=\textsf{tmp}&\implies&r=r^{\prime})&~\land~\\
&(k=\textsf{var}&\implies&r=r^{\prime})&~\land~\\
&(k=\textsf{paused}&\implies&\rho\vdash r^{\prime}<r)&~\land~\\
&(k=\textsf{iso}&\implies&r\neq r^{\prime}~\land~(r^{\prime}\in\mathit{Cl}~\lor~((\rho\vdash r<r^{\prime})~\lor~r^{\prime}\in\mathit{Fr}~\land~r\in\mathit{Fr})))&~\land~\\
&(k=\textsf{imm}&\implies&r^{\prime}\in\mathit{Fr}))&\end{aligned}

\mathsf{location\_ok}(\mathcal{G})\iff\forall\mathit{ref}\in\mathcal{G}.\mathsf{location\_ok}1(\mathit{ref})

\displaystyle\mathsf{location\_ok}1(

\displaystyle\mathit{loc}\xrightarrow{f,k}\mathit{loc}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\iff

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &(k=\textsf{mut}&\implies&\mathit{loc}^{\prime}=\mathtt{heap}(r^{\prime},\iota^{\prime}))&~\land~\\
&(k=\textsf{tmp}&\implies&((\mathit{loc}=\mathtt{root}(r)~\lor~\mathit{loc}=\mathtt{temp}(r,\iota))~\land~\mathit{loc}^{\prime}=\mathtt{temp}(r^{\prime},\iota^{\prime})))&~\land~\\
&(k=\textsf{var}&\implies&(\mathit{loc}=\mathtt{root}(r)~\land~\mathit{loc}^{\prime}=\mathtt{temp}(r^{\prime},\iota^{\prime})))&~\land~\\
&(k=\textsf{paused}&\implies&((\mathit{loc}=\mathtt{root}(r)~\lor~\mathit{loc}=\mathtt{temp}(r,\iota))~\land~\mathit{loc}^{\prime}\neq\mathtt{root}(r^{\prime})))&~\land~\\
&(k=\textsf{iso}&\implies&\mathit{loc}^{\prime}=\mathtt{heap}(r^{\prime},\iota^{\prime}))&~\land~\\
&(k=\textsf{imm}&\implies&\mathit{loc}^{\prime}=\mathtt{heap}(r^{\prime},\iota^{\prime}))&\end{aligned}

\mathsf{deep\_freeze}(\mathit{Fr},\mathcal{G})\iff\forall\mathit{ref}\in\mathcal{G}.\mathsf{deep\_freeze}1(\mathit{Fr},\mathit{ref})

\mathsf{deep\_freeze}1(\mathit{Fr},\mathit{loc}[r]\xrightarrow{\_,\_}\mathit{loc}^{\prime}[r^{\prime}])\iff r\in\mathit{Fr}\implies r^{\prime}\in\mathit{Fr}

\mathsf{var\_unique}(\mathcal{G})\iff\begin{aligned} \forall&\mathit{ref}_{1},Ref_{2}\in\mathcal{G}.\\
&\mathsf{var\_pw\_unique}(\mathit{ref}_{1},\mathit{ref}_{2})\end{aligned}

\mathsf{var\_pw\_unique}(\mathit{ref}_{1},\mathit{ref}_{2})\iff\begin{aligned} &\mathit{ref}_{1}=\_\xrightarrow{\_,\textsf{var}}\mathit{loc}~\land~&\\
&\mathit{ref}_{2}=\_\xrightarrow{\_,\_}\mathit{loc}&\implies\mathit{ref}_{1}=\mathit{ref}_{2}\end{aligned}

C.3.4.
Topology Invariant

Finally we define
\mathsf{topology\_ok}(\mathit{rcfg})
. We again use the graph representation to
formulate it.

\displaystyle\mathsf{topology\_ok}(

\displaystyle\overline{\Gamma},\mathit{rcfg})\iff

\displaystyle\mathsf{topology\_ok\_graph}(\rho(\overline{\Gamma},\mathit{rcfg}),\mathit{Frozen}(\mathit{rcfg}),G(\mathit{rcfg}))~\land~

\displaystyle\mathsf{entrypoints\_ok}(\rho(\overline{\Gamma},\mathit{rcfg}),G(\mathit{rcfg}))

\displaystyle\mathsf{topology\_ok\_graph}(\rho,\mathit{Fr},\mathcal{G})

\displaystyle\iff

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \forall&\mathit{ref}_{1},\mathit{ref}_{2}\in\mathcal{G}.\\
&\mathsf{topology\_pw\_ok}(\rho,\mathit{Fr},\mathit{ref}_{1},\mathit{ref}_{2})\end{aligned}

\displaystyle\mathsf{topology\_pw\_ok}(

\displaystyle\rho,\mathit{Fr},\mathit{ref}_{1},\mathit{ref}_{2})\iff

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \mathit{ref}_{1}=&~\mathit{loc}_{1}[r_{1}]\xrightarrow{\_,\_}\mathit{loc}_{1}^{\prime}[r_{1}^{\prime}]~\land~\\
\mathit{ref}_{2}=&~\mathit{loc}_{2}[r_{2}]\xrightarrow{\_,\_}\mathit{loc}_{2}^{\prime}[r_{2}^{\prime}]\\
\implies&\begin{aligned} &\mathit{ref}_{1}=\mathit{ref}_{2}&~\lor~\\
&r_{1}^{\prime}\neq r_{2}^{\prime}&~\lor~\\
&r_{1}^{\prime}\in\mathit{Fr}~\lor~r_{2}^{\prime}\in\mathit{Fr}&~\lor~\\
&\rho\vdash r_{1}^{\prime}\leq r_{1}~\lor~\rho\vdash r_{2}^{\prime}\leq r_{2}&\end{aligned}\end{aligned}

Note that the topology invariant in the paper also relies on the source of
backward pointing references being
\mathtt{temp}
locations. This is not included in
this version, but still holds because of
\mathsf{capability\_ok}
.

\displaystyle\mathsf{entrypoints\_ok}(r^{\prime}\underset{x.f}{::}r::\rho,\mathcal{G})\iff

\displaystyle\mathtt{root}(r)\xrightarrow{x,k_{x}}\mathit{loc}_{x}[r^{*}]\in\mathcal{G}~\land~

\displaystyle\mathit{loc}_{x}[r^{*}]\xrightarrow{f,k_{f}}\mathtt{heap}(r^{\prime},\_)\in\mathcal{G}~\land~

\displaystyle\mathsf{entrypoints\_ok}(r::\rho,\mathcal{G})

\displaystyle\mathsf{entrypoints\_ok}(r::\varepsilon,\mathcal{G})\iff\text{true}

Appendix D
Effects

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\mathit{use}&::=&x~|~\texttt{{drop}}~x\\
\mathit{Eff}&::=&\textit{load}(x,y.f)\\
&~|&\textit{swap}(x,y.f,\mathit{use})\\
&~|&\textit{bind}(\overline{x=\mathit{use}})\\
&~|&\textit{halloc}(x,k,\#C,\overline{\mathit{use}})\\
&~|&\textit{salloc}(x,k,\mathsf{\#}\mathit{CL},\overline{\mathit{use}})\\
&~|&\textit{enter}(w,k,y.f,CL,\overline{x=\mathit{use}})\\
&~|&\textit{badenter}(x.f)\\
&~|&\textit{exit}(x,\mathit{use},y.f,w.g)\\
&~|&\textit{cast}(x,\mathit{use},k~C)\\
&~|&\textit{nocast}(x,\mathit{use},k~C)\\
&~|&\textit{bind}(\overline{x=\mathit{use}})\\
&~|&\epsilon\\
\end{array}

D.1.
Well-Formed Effects

We use the following helper predicate for a capability pointing
into an open region:

\mathbf{open}(k)\iff k\in\{\textsf{mut},\textsf{tmp},\textsf{var},\textsf{paused}\}

Effects are typed under a stack of contexts
\overline{\Gamma}
, defined in
Section
E.2
.

\overline{\Gamma}\vdash\mathit{Eff}\dashv\overline{\Gamma}

(Well-formed Effects)

\frac{\mathit{w}\,\mathit{fresh}\forall\mathit{i}\in[1,\mathit{n}].~\mathit{x_{\mathit{i}}}\,\mathit{fresh}\forall\mathit{i}\in[1,\mathit{n}].~\Gamma_{\mathit{i}}\vdash\mathit{use_{\mathit{i}}}:\mathit{t_{\mathit{i}}}\dashv\Gamma_{i+1}\quad\quad\Gamma_{n+1}(\mathit{y})=\mathit{k^{\prime}}~\mathit{CL}\textsf{open}(\mathit{k^{\prime}})\mathbf{ftype}\,(\mathit{CL},\mathit{f})=\mathit{t}\textsf{cap}(\textsf{iso},\mathit{t})\quad\quad\mathit{k}\in\{\textsf{tmp},\textsf{var}\}\mathit{k}=\textsf{var}\Rightarrow\mathit{k^{\prime}}=\textsf{var}\mathit{t^{\prime}}=\mathit{make\_mut}(\mathit{t})\quad\quad\Gamma=\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\mathit{t^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\,...\,,\mathit{y_{\mathit{n}}}:\mathit{t^{\prime}_{\mathit{n}}}\text{~~where~~}\mathit{t^{\prime}_{\mathit{i}}}=\begin{cases}\mathit{t_{\mathit{i}}}&\text{if }\textsf{cap}(\textsf{iso},\mathit{t_{\mathit{i}}})\quad\textsf{paused}\mathop{\odot}\mathit{t_{\mathit{i}}}&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}\quad\quad\Gamma^{\prime}=\Gamma,\mathit{z}:\mathit{k}~\texttt{Cell}[\mathit{t^{\prime}}]}{\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}::\overline{\Gamma}\vdash\mathit{enter}\,(\mathit{w},\mathit{k},\mathit{y}.\mathit{f},\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}=\mathit{use_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\,...\,,\mathit{x_{\mathit{n}}}=\mathit{use_{\mathit{n}}})\dashv\Gamma^{\prime}::\Gamma_{n+1}::\overline{\Gamma}}\quad\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={\hypertarget{ottalt:rule:ott:wf-eff-enter}{{{wf-eff-enter}}}
}{},}\frac{\Gamma(\mathit{y})=\mathit{k}~\mathit{CL}\textsf{open}(\mathit{k})\quad\quad\mathbf{ftype}\,(\mathit{CL},\mathit{f})=\mathit{t}\textsf{cap}(\textsf{iso},\mathit{t})}{\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}::\overline{\Gamma}\vdash\textit{badenter}(\mathit{y}.\mathit{f})\dashv\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}::\overline{\Gamma}}\quad\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={\hypertarget{ottalt:rule:ott:wf-eff-badenter}{{{wf-eff-badenter}}}

}{},}\frac{\mathit{x}\,\mathit{fresh}\Gamma\vdash\mathit{use}:\mathit{t}\dashv\Gamma^{\prime}\textsf{cap}(\{\textsf{var}\}^{c},\mathit{t})\Gamma^{\prime}(\mathit{y})=\mathit{k}~\mathit{CL}\quad\quad\mathit{k}\in\{\textsf{mut},\textsf{tmp}\}\mathbf{ftype}\,(\mathit{CL},\mathit{f})=\mathit{t}}{\Gamma::\overline{\Gamma}\vdash\mathit{swap}\,(\mathit{x},\mathit{y}.\mathit{f},\mathit{use})\dashv\Gamma^{\prime},\mathit{x}:\mathit{t}::\overline{\Gamma}}\quad\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={\hypertarget{ottalt:rule:ott:wf-eff-swap-class}{{{wf-eff-swap-class}}}

}{},}\frac{\mathit{x}\,\mathit{fresh}\mathit{k}\in\{\textsf{tmp},\textsf{var}\}\vdash\mathit{k}~\mathit{CL}\mathbf{ftypes}\,(C)=\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\,...\,,\mathit{f_{\mathit{n}}}:\mathit{t_{\mathit{n}}}\quad\quad\forall\mathit{i}\in[1,\mathit{n}].~\Gamma_{\mathit{i}}\vdash\mathit{use_{\mathit{i}}}:\mathit{t_{\mathit{i}}}\dashv\Gamma_{i+1}}{\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}::\overline{\Gamma}\vdash\textit{salloc}(\mathit{x},\mathit{k},\#CL,\mathit{use_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\,...\,,\mathit{use_{\mathit{n}}})\dashv\Gamma_{n+1},\mathit{x}:\mathit{k}~\mathit{CL}::\overline{\Gamma}}\quad\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={\hypertarget{ottalt:rule:ott:wf-eff-salloc}{{{wf-eff-salloc}}}
}{},}\frac{\mathit{x}\,\mathit{fresh}\Gamma\vdash\mathit{use}:\mathit{t}\dashv\Gamma^{\prime}\textsf{cap}(\textsf{iso},\mathit{t})}{\Gamma::\overline{\Gamma}\vdash\textit{freeze}(\mathit{x},\mathit{use})\dashv\Gamma^{\prime},\mathit{x}:\mathit{make\_imm}(\mathit{t})::\overline{\Gamma}}\quad\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={\hypertarget{ottalt:rule:ott:wf-eff-freeze}{{{wf-eff-freeze}}}

}{},}\frac{\mathit{x}\,\mathit{fresh}\Gamma\vdash\mathit{use}:\mathit{t}\dashv\Gamma^{\prime}}{\Gamma::\overline{\Gamma}\vdash\textit{nocast}(\mathit{x},\mathit{use},\mathit{k}~C)\dashv\Gamma^{\prime},\mathit{x}:\mathit{t}::\overline{\Gamma}}\quad\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={\hypertarget{ottalt:rule:ott:wf-eff-nocast}{{{wf-eff-nocast}}}
}{},}\frac{\forall\mathit{i}\in[1,\mathit{n}].~\mathit{x_{\mathit{i}}}\,\mathit{fresh}\forall\mathit{i}\in[1,\mathit{n}].~\Gamma_{\mathit{i}}\vdash\mathit{use_{\mathit{i}}}:\mathit{t_{\mathit{i}}}\dashv\Gamma_{i+1}}{\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}::\overline{\Gamma}\vdash\textit{bind}(\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}=\mathit{use_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\,...\,,\mathit{x_{\mathit{n}}}=\mathit{use_{\mathit{n}}})\dashv\Gamma_{n+1},\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\,...\,,\mathit{x_{\mathit{n}}}:\mathit{t_{\mathit{n}}}::\overline{\Gamma}}\quad\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={\hypertarget{ottalt:rule:ott:wf-eff-bind}{{{wf-eff-bind}}}
}{},}\frac{\overline{\Gamma}[\mathit{x}:\mathit{t}]\vdash\mathit{Eff}\dashv\overline{\Gamma}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\overline{\Gamma}[\mathit{x}:\mathit{t^{\prime}}]\vdash\mathit{Eff}\dashv\overline{\Gamma}_{{\mathrm{2}}}}{\overline{\Gamma}[\mathit{x}:\mathit{t}\mathop{|}\mathit{t^{\prime}}]\vdash\mathit{Eff}\dashv\overline{\Gamma}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\mathop{|}\overline{\Gamma}_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\quad\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={\hypertarget{ottalt:rule:ott:wf-eff-split}{{{wf-eff-split}}}
}{},}\frac{}{\overline{\Gamma}\vdash\epsilon\dashv\overline{\Gamma}}\quad\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={\hypertarget{ottalt:rule:ott:wf-eff-eps}{{{wf-eff-eps}}}

Appendix E
Command Semantics

{{{\begin{array}[]{rcll}e&::=&\mathit{use}~|~\texttt{{let}}~x=b~\texttt{{in}}~e~|~\texttt{{if}}~\texttt{{typetest}}(\mathit{use},t)\texttt{\char 123\relax}y=>e\texttt{\char 125\relax}\texttt{\char 123\relax}y=>e\texttt{\char 125\relax}&\text{Expressions}\\
\mathit{use}&::=&x~|~\texttt{{drop}}~x&\text{Variable usage}\\
b&::=&\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@language\tt\textasteriskcentered}}}\mathit{lval}~|~\mathit{lval}~\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@language\tt:=}}}~\mathit{use}~|~\mathit{fnc}(\overline{use})~|~\texttt{{var}}~\mathit{use}~|~\texttt{{new}}~k~C(\overline{use})&\text{Bound expressions}\\
&~|&\texttt{{enter}}~\mathit{lval}~[\overline{y=\mathit{use}}]\texttt{\char 123\relax}z~\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@language\tt=\textgreater}}}~e\texttt{\char 125\relax}~|~\texttt{{freeze}}~\mathit{use}~|~\texttt{{merge}}~\mathit{use}~|~e\\
\mathit{lval}&::=&x~|~x.f&\text{L-values}\\
de&::=&e~|~\texttt{{let}}~x=db~\texttt{{in}}~e~|~\textbf{Failure}&\text{Dynamic expressions}\\
db&::=&b~|~de~|~\texttt{{entered}}~x.f~y.f^{\prime}\texttt{\char 123\relax}de\texttt{\char 125\relax}&\text{Dynamic bound expressions}\\
de[\bullet]&::=&\bullet~|~\texttt{{let}}~x=\bullet~\texttt{{in}}~e~|~\texttt{{let}}~x=\texttt{{entered}}~y.f~w.f^{\prime}\texttt{\char 123\relax}\bullet\texttt{\char 125\relax}~\texttt{{in}}~e&\text{Execution context}\\
\mathit{ccfg}&::=&\texttt{\char 123\relax}de\texttt{\char 125\relax}&\text{Dynamic configuration}\end{array}

The syntax of the command language is in A-normal form (ANF).
An expression is a
\mathit{use}
of a name (a potentially destructive
read), the binding of an expression
b
to a name, or a dynamic
type test, where the value is rebound in the chosen branch in
order to track the type.
Note that we model mutable variables as objects, created with the
syntax
\texttt{{var}}~\mathit{use}
. A bound expression is a field access, a
field update, a function call, the creation of a variable or an
object, an
enter
block, or a
freeze
or
merge
.
An enter block enters through a variable
x
or a field
x.f
and
explicitly captures variables
\overline{\mathit{use}}
, potentially
destructively, as
\overline{y}
. The bridge object is bound to
z

and the body
e
of the block is executed. The capture list is for
convenience only, and could be inferred based on variable usage in

e
.

During execution, an
enter
block reduces to a
entered

block, which stores the field entered through and the field in
which the new bridge object can be found when exiting the region.
The only difference between dynamic expressions
de
and static
expressions
e
is that dynamic expressions can contain

entered
blocks in the currently bound expression being
evaluated, or denote failure.
We use an execution context
de[\bullet]
to find the inner-most
dynamic expression to evaluate.
A dynamic configuration is just a dynamic expression.

We assume the existence of a table of well-formed functions such
that if

\mathbf{fnctype}(\mathit{fnc})=(t_{1},...,t_{n})\rightarrow t
,
then

\mathbf{fnclookup}(\mathit{fnc})=(x_{1},...,x_{n})\rightarrow e

and
x_{1}:t_{1},...,x_{n}:t_{n}\vdash e:t\dashv\Gamma^{\prime}
for
some
\Gamma^{\prime}
. We further assume that function lookup renames all
let-bound variables to globally fresh names to avoid accidental
shadowing.

E.1.
Dynamic Semantics

Because the command language is in ANF, many rules differ only in
which effect they emit. In order to avoid duplicating rules, for
expressions with the shape
\texttt{{let}}~x~=~e_{1}~\texttt{{in}}~e_{2}
we define a
helper function to calculate the effect of
b
:

{{{{\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathbf{effect}(x,\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@language\tt\textasteriskcentered{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{y}}}.{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{f}}}}}})&=&\textit{load}(x,y.f)\\
\mathbf{effect}(x,\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@language\tt\textasteriskcentered{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{y}}}}}})&=&\textit{load}(x,y.val)\\
\mathbf{effect}(x,\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@language\tt{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{y}}}.{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{f}}}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_space}{}}:=}}}\mathit{use})&=&\textit{swap}(x,y.f,\mathit{use})\\
\mathbf{effect}(x,\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@language\tt{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{y}}}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_space}{}}:=}}}\mathit{use})&=&\textit{swap}(x,y.val,\mathit{use})\\
\mathbf{effect}(x,\texttt{{new}}~\textsf{mut}~C(\overline{\mathit{use}}))&=&\textit{halloc}(x,\textsf{mut},\#C,\overline{\mathit{use}})\\
\mathbf{effect}(x,\texttt{{new}}~\textsf{iso}~C(\overline{\mathit{use}}))&=&\textit{halloc}(x,\textsf{iso},\#C,\overline{\mathit{use}})\\
\mathbf{effect}(x,\texttt{{new}}~\textsf{tmp}~C(\overline{\mathit{use}}))&=&\textit{salloc}(x,\textsf{tmp},\#C,\overline{\mathit{use}})\\
\mathbf{effect}(x,\texttt{{var}}~\mathit{use})&=&\textit{salloc}(x,\textsf{var},\#\texttt{Cell},\mathit{use})\\
\mathbf{effect}(x,\texttt{{freeze}}~\mathit{use})&=&\textit{freeze}(x,\mathit{use})\\
\mathbf{effect}(x,\texttt{{merge}}~\mathit{use})&=&\textit{merge}(x,\mathit{use})\\
\mathbf{effect}(x,\mathit{use})&=&\textit{bind}(x=\mathit{use})\\
\end{array}

de\xrightarrow{\mathit{Eff}}de

(Command semantics)

\frac{\mathit{x^{\prime}}\,\mathit{fresh}\mathbf{effect}(\mathit{x^{\prime}},\mathit{db})=\mathit{Eff}}{\texttt{{let}}\,\,\mathit{x}=\mathit{db}\,\texttt{{in}}\,\,\mathit{e}\,\xrightarrow{\mathit{Eff}}\,\mathit{e}[\mathit{x}\mapsto\mathit{x^{\prime}}]}\quad\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={\hypertarget{ottalt:rule:ott:cmd-let-eff}{{{cmd-let-eff}}}
}{},}\frac{}{\texttt{{let}}\,\,\mathit{x}=\texttt{{enter}}~\mathit{y}.\mathit{f}~[\mathit{z_{{\mathrm{1}}}}=\mathit{use_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\,...\,,\mathit{z_{\mathit{n}}}=\mathit{use_{\mathit{n}}}]~\{\mathit{w}~\texttt{=>}~\mathit{e}\}\,\texttt{{in}}\,\,\mathit{e^{\prime}}\,\xrightarrow{\textit{badenter}(\mathit{y}.\mathit{f})}\,\mathbf{Failure}}\quad\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={\hypertarget{ottalt:rule:ott:cmd-enter-field-fail}{{{cmd-enter-field-fail}}}
}{},}\frac{}{\texttt{{let}}\,\,\mathit{x}=\texttt{{enter}}~\mathit{y}~[\mathit{z_{{\mathrm{1}}}}=\mathit{use_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\,...\,,\mathit{z_{\mathit{n}}}=\mathit{use_{\mathit{n}}}]~\{\mathit{w}~\texttt{=>}~\mathit{e}\}\,\texttt{{in}}\,\,\mathit{e^{\prime}}\,\xrightarrow{\textit{badenter}(\mathit{y}.\texttt{val})}\,\mathbf{Failure}}\quad\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={\hypertarget{ottalt:rule:ott:cmd-enter-var-fail}{{{cmd-enter-var-fail}}}
}{},}\frac{\mathit{db_{{\mathrm{1}}}}=\texttt{{enter}}~\mathit{y}.\mathit{f}~[\mathit{z_{{\mathrm{1}}}}=\mathit{use_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\,...\,,\mathit{z_{\mathit{n}}}=\mathit{use_{\mathit{n}}}]~\{\mathit{w}~\texttt{=>}~\mathit{e}\}\quad\quad\mathit{db_{{\mathrm{2}}}}=\mathbf{entered}~\mathit{y}.\mathit{f}~\mathit{w^{\prime}}.\texttt{val}~\{\mathit{e}[\mathit{w}\mapsto\mathit{w^{\prime}},\mathit{z_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\mapsto\mathit{z^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\,...\,,\mathit{z_{\mathit{n}}}\mapsto\mathit{z^{\prime}_{\mathit{n}}}]\}\quad\quad\mathit{w^{\prime}}\,\mathit{fresh}\mathit{z^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\,...\,,\mathit{z^{\prime}_{\mathit{n}}}\,\mathit{fresh}}{\texttt{{let}}\,\,\mathit{x}=\mathit{db_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\,\texttt{{in}}\,\,\mathit{e^{\prime}}\,\xrightarrow{\mathit{enter}\,(\mathit{w^{\prime}},\textsf{tmp},\mathit{y}.\mathit{f},\mathit{z^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}=\mathit{use_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\,...\,,\mathit{z^{\prime}_{\mathit{n}}}=\mathit{use_{\mathit{n}}})}\,\texttt{{let}}\,\,\mathit{x}=\mathit{db_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\,\texttt{{in}}\,\,\mathit{e^{\prime}}}\quad\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={\hypertarget{ottalt:rule:ott:cmd-enter-field}{{{cmd-enter-field}}}
}{},}\frac{\mathit{db_{{\mathrm{1}}}}=\texttt{{enter}}~\mathit{y}~[\mathit{z_{{\mathrm{1}}}}=\mathit{use_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\,...\,,\mathit{z_{\mathit{n}}}=\mathit{use_{\mathit{n}}}]~\{\mathit{w}~\texttt{=>}~\mathit{e}\}\quad\quad\mathit{db_{{\mathrm{2}}}}=\mathbf{entered}~\mathit{y}.\mathit{f}~\mathit{w^{\prime}}.\texttt{val}~\{\mathit{e}[\mathit{w}\mapsto\mathit{w^{\prime}},\mathit{z_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\mapsto\mathit{z^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\,...\,,\mathit{z_{\mathit{n}}}\mapsto\mathit{z^{\prime}_{\mathit{n}}}]\}\quad\quad\mathit{w^{\prime}}\,\mathit{fresh}\mathit{z^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\,...\,,\mathit{z^{\prime}_{\mathit{n}}}\,\mathit{fresh}}{\texttt{{let}}\,\,\mathit{x}=\mathit{db_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\,\texttt{{in}}\,\,\mathit{e^{\prime}}\,\xrightarrow{\mathit{enter}\,(\mathit{w^{\prime}},\textsf{var},\mathit{y}.\texttt{val},\mathit{z^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}=\mathit{use_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\,...\,,\mathit{z^{\prime}_{\mathit{n}}}=\mathit{use_{\mathit{n}}})}\,\texttt{{let}}\,\,\mathit{x}=\mathit{db_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\,\texttt{{in}}\,\,\mathit{e^{\prime}}}\quad\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={\hypertarget{ottalt:rule:ott:cmd-enter-var}{{{cmd-enter-var}}}
}{},}\frac{\mathit{x^{\prime}}\,\mathit{fresh}}{\texttt{{let}}\,\,\mathit{x}=\mathbf{entered}~\mathit{y}.\mathit{f}~\mathit{w^{\prime}}.\texttt{val}~\{\mathit{use}\}\,\texttt{{in}}\,\,\mathit{e}\,\xrightarrow{\mathit{exit}\,(\mathit{x^{\prime}},\mathit{use},\mathit{y}.\mathit{f},\mathit{w^{\prime}}.\texttt{val})}\,\mathit{e}[\mathit{x}\mapsto\mathit{x^{\prime}}]}\quad\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={\hypertarget{ottalt:rule:ott:cmd-exit}{{{cmd-exit}}}
}{},}\frac{\mathit{k}~\mathit{CL}<:\mathit{t}\mathit{y^{\prime}}\,\mathit{fresh}}{\texttt{{if}}\,\,\texttt{{typetest}}\,\,(\mathit{use},\mathit{t})\,\texttt{\char 123\relax}\,\mathit{y}\mathop{\texttt{=>}}\mathit{e_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\,\texttt{\char 125\relax}\,\,\texttt{\char 123\relax}\,\mathit{y}\mathop{\texttt{=>}}\mathit{e_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\,\texttt{\char 125\relax}\,\,\xrightarrow{\textit{cast}(\mathit{y^{\prime}},\mathit{use},\mathit{k}~\mathit{CL})}\,\mathit{e_{{\mathrm{1}}}}[\mathit{y}\mapsto\mathit{y^{\prime}}]}\quad\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={\hypertarget{ottalt:rule:ott:cmd-if-typetest-true}{{{cmd-if-typetest-true}}}
}{},}\frac{\mathit{k}~\mathit{CL}\not<:\mathit{t}\mathit{y^{\prime}}\,\mathit{fresh}}{\texttt{{if}}\,\,\texttt{{typetest}}\,\,(\mathit{use},\mathit{t})\,\texttt{\char 123\relax}\,\mathit{y}\mathop{\texttt{=>}}\mathit{e_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\,\texttt{\char 125\relax}\,\,\texttt{\char 123\relax}\,\mathit{y}\mathop{\texttt{=>}}\mathit{e_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\,\texttt{\char 125\relax}\,\,\xrightarrow{\textit{nocast}(\mathit{y^{\prime}},\mathit{use},\mathit{k}~\mathit{CL})}\,\mathit{e_{{\mathrm{2}}}}[\mathit{y}\mapsto\mathit{y^{\prime}}]}\quad\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={\hypertarget{ottalt:rule:ott:cmd-if-typetest-false}{{{cmd-if-typetest-false}}}
}{},}\frac{\mathbf{fnclookup}\,(\mathit{fnc})=(\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\,...\,,\mathit{x_{\mathit{n}}})\rightarrow\mathit{e}_{\mathit{body}}\mathit{x^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\,...\,,\mathit{x^{\prime}_{\mathit{n}}}\,\mathit{fresh}\quad\quad\overline{x}=\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\,...\,,\mathit{x_{\mathit{n}}}\overline{x}^{\prime}=\mathit{x^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\,...\,,\mathit{x^{\prime}_{\mathit{n}}}}{\texttt{{let}}\,\,\mathit{x}=\mathit{fnc}(\mathit{use_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\,...\,,\mathit{use_{\mathit{n}}})\,\texttt{{in}}\,\,\mathit{e}\,\xrightarrow{\textit{bind}(\mathit{x^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}=\mathit{use_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\,...\,,\mathit{x^{\prime}_{\mathit{n}}}=\mathit{use_{\mathit{n}}})}\,\texttt{{let}}\,\,\mathit{x}=\mathit{e}_{\mathit{body}}[\overline{x}\mapsto\overline{x}^{\prime}]\,\texttt{{in}}\,\,\mathit{e}}\quad\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={\hypertarget{ottalt:rule:ott:cmd-call}{{{cmd-call}}}
}{},}\frac{\mathit{de^{\prime}}\,\xrightarrow{\mathit{Eff}}\,\mathit{de^{\prime\prime}}}{\mathit{de}[\mathit{de^{\prime}}]\,\xrightarrow{\mathit{Eff}}\,\mathit{de}[\mathit{de^{\prime\prime}}]}\quad\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={\hypertarget{ottalt:rule:ott:cmd-ec}{{{cmd-ec}}}
}{},}\frac{}{\mathit{de}[\mathbf{Failure}]\,\xrightarrow{\epsilon}\,\mathbf{Failure}}\quad\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={\hypertarget{ottalt:rule:ott:cmd-ec-fail}{{{cmd-ec-fail}}}
}{},}

E.2.
Static Semantics

{{\begin{array}[]{rcll}t&::=&k~C~|~k~\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@language\tt{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{Cell}}}}}}[t]~|~t\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@language\tt\textbar}}}t&\text{Types}\\
\Gamma&::=&\Gamma,x:t~|~\Gamma,x:\mathbf{undef}~|~\varepsilon&\text{Typing context}\\
\overline{\Gamma}&::=&\overline{\Gamma}\underset{x.f}{::}\Gamma~|~\varepsilon&\text{Dynamic typing context}\end{array}

A type is a class
C
with a capability
k
, a
Cell
with a
given type (
c.f.
, ML-style ref cells), or a union
{t_{1}\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@language\tt\textbar}}}t_{2}
of
two types.
A typing context maps variables to types, or remembers that they
have been consumed.
When typing a dynamic expression, we use a stack of typing
contexts mirroring the stack of open regions. Each
::
between
two contexts in the stack is marked with
x.f
denoting the field
through which we entered the next region. Whenever we don’t care
about this field, we omit it for readability.
When dealing with union types, we sometimes need to merge two
different contexts with the same domain but (possibly) different
codomains. This is done with the following function:

{{{{{{(\Gamma_{1}\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@language\tt\textbar}}}\Gamma_{2})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{llcl}(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@language\tt\textbar}}}\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}),x:t_{1}\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@language\tt\textbar}}}t_{2}&\text{if }\Gamma_{1}=\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},x:t_{1}&\text{ and }&\Gamma_{2}=\Gamma_{2}^{\prime},x:t_{2}\\
(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@language\tt\textbar}}}\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}),x:\mathbf{undef}&\text{if }\Gamma_{1}=\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},x:\mathbf{undef}&\text{ and }&\Gamma_{2}=\Gamma_{2}^{\prime},x:t_{2}\\
(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@language\tt\textbar}}}\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}),x:\mathbf{undef}&\text{if }\Gamma_{1}=\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},x:t_{1}&\text{ and }&\Gamma_{2}=\Gamma_{2}^{\prime},x:\mathbf{undef}\\
(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@language\tt\textbar}}}\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}),x:\mathbf{undef}&\text{if }\Gamma_{1}=\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},x:\mathbf{undef}&\text{ and }&\Gamma_{2}=\Gamma_{2}^{\prime},x:\mathbf{undef}\\
\varepsilon&\text{if }\Gamma_{1}=\varepsilon&\text{ and }&\Gamma_{2}=\varepsilon\end{array}\right.

{{{(\overline{\Gamma}_{1}\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@language\tt\textbar}}}\overline{\Gamma}_{2})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{llcl}(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@language\tt\textbar}}}\Gamma_{2}^{\prime})::(\overline{\Gamma}_{1}^{\prime}\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@language\tt\textbar}}}\overline{\Gamma}_{2}^{\prime})&\text{if }\overline{\Gamma}_{1}=\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}::\overline{\Gamma_{1}}^{\prime}&\text{ and }&\overline{\Gamma}_{2}=\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}::\overline{\Gamma}_{2}^{\prime}\\
\varepsilon&\text{if }\overline{\Gamma}_{1}=\varepsilon&\text{ and }&\overline{\Gamma}_{2}=\varepsilon\end{array}\right.

We sometimes need to compare what variables are defined for a certain
\Gamma

or
\overline{\Gamma}
. We use define
\mathbf{defined}
as follows

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathbf{defined}(\Gamma,x:t)&=&\mathbf{defined}(\Gamma)\cup\{x\}\\
\mathbf{defined}(\Gamma,x:\mathbf{undef})&=&\mathbf{defined}(\Gamma)\\
\mathbf{defined}(\varepsilon)&=&\emptyset\\
\end{array}

To compare
\overline{\Gamma}_{1}
and
\overline{\Gamma}_{2}
, we check pairwise set inclusion of
defined sets:

\displaystyle\Gamma::\overline{\Gamma}

\displaystyle\mathop{\overset{\mathsf{pw}}{\subseteq}}\Gamma^{\prime}::\overline{\Gamma}^{\prime}\iff

\displaystyle\mathbf{defined}(\Gamma)\subseteq\mathbf{defined}(\Gamma^{\prime})~\land~

\displaystyle\overline{\Gamma}\mathop{\overset{\mathsf{pw}}{\subseteq}}\overline{\Gamma}^{\prime}

We further use the following helper functions and predicates
(viewpoint adaptation is in Table
4
):

We use
\mathit{make\_iso}
and
\mathit{make\_mut}
to change all
occurrences of
mut
to
iso
and vice versa for a type (all
other capabilities remain unchanged).

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\mathit{make\_iso}(k~CL)&=&\begin{cases}\textsf{iso}~CL&\text{if }k=\textsf{mut}\\
k~CL&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}\\
\mathit{make\_iso}(t_{1}~|~t_{2})&=&\mathit{make\_iso}(t_{1})~|~\mathit{make\_iso}(t_{2})\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\mathit{make\_mut}(k~CL)&=&\begin{cases}\textsf{mut}~CL&\text{if }k=\textsf{iso}\\
k~CL&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}\\
\mathit{make\_mut}(t_{1}~|~t_{2})&=&\mathit{make\_mut}(t_{1})~|~\mathit{make\_mut}(t_{2})\end{array}

We use the predicate
\mathsf{cap}(\overline{k},t)
to check if all
capabilities in a type are in
\overline{k}
. We omit brackets for
singleton sets (the first case below), and use the complement
notation
\mathsf{cap}(\{k\}^{c},t)
for checking that capability
k
does

not
occur anywhere in
t
.

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\mathsf{cap}(k,t)&\equiv&\mathsf{cap}(\{k\},t)\\
\mathsf{cap}(\overline{k},k^{\prime}CL)&\iff&k^{\prime}\in\overline{k}\\
\mathsf{cap}(\overline{k},t_{1}~|~t_{2})&\iff&\mathsf{cap}(\overline{k},t_{1})\land\mathsf{cap}(\overline{k},t_{2})\end{array}

We use
\mathbf{classtype}(t)
to check if a type is definitely a
class type:

\mathbf{classtype}(t)\iff\forall k~CL\in t.~\exists C.CL=C

,

\alpha

\begin{array}[]{rcl}k\mathop{\odot}(k^{\prime}~CL)&=&\begin{cases}k^{\prime\prime}~CL&\text{if }k\mathop{\odot}k^{\prime}=k^{\prime\prime}\\

We extend viewpoint adaptation to
k\mathop{\odot}\Gamma
and
k\mathop{\odot}F
by mapping
over the elements of
\Gamma
and
F
respectively.

\Gamma\vdash e\dashv\Gamma\quad\Gamma\vdash b\dashv\Gamma

(Command Language Typing)

\frac{\vdash\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}(\mathit{x})=\mathit{k}~\mathit{CL}\quad\quad\mathit{k}\neq\textsf{iso}\mathit{k}\neq\textsf{var}}{\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash\mathit{x}:\mathit{k}~\mathit{CL}\dashv\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\quad\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={\hypertarget{ottalt:rule:ott:cmd-ty-use-keep}{{{cmd-ty-use-keep}}}
}{},}\frac{\vdash\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}=\Gamma[\mathit{x}:\mathit{t}]\quad\quad\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}=\Gamma[\mathit{x}:\,\mathbf{undef}\,]}{\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash\texttt{{drop}}\,\,\mathit{x}:\mathit{t}\dashv\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\quad\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={\hypertarget{ottalt:rule:ott:cmd-ty-use-drop}{{{cmd-ty-use-drop}}}
}{},}\frac{\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash\mathit{b}:\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\dashv\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\mathit{x}\,\not\in\,\mathit{dom}\,(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}})\quad\quad\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}},\mathit{x}:\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\vdash\mathit{e}:\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\dashv\Gamma_{{\mathrm{3}}},\mathit{x}:\mathbf{\_}}{\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash\texttt{{let}}\,\,\mathit{x}=\mathit{b}\,\texttt{{in}}\,\,\mathit{e}:\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\dashv\Gamma_{{\mathrm{3}}}}\quad\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={\hypertarget{ottalt:rule:ott:cmd-ty-let}{{{cmd-ty-let}}}
}{},}\frac{\Gamma\vdash\mathit{use}:\mathit{t^{\prime}}\dashv\Gamma^{\prime}\mathbf{classtype}\,(\mathit{t})\quad\quad\Gamma^{\prime},\mathit{y}:\mathit{t}\vdash\mathit{e_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\dashv\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{y}:\mathbf{\_}\Gamma^{\prime},\mathit{y}:\mathit{t^{\prime}}\vdash\mathit{e_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\dashv\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}},\mathit{y}:\mathbf{\_}}{\Gamma\vdash\texttt{{if}}\,\,\texttt{{typetest}}\,\,(\mathit{use},\mathit{t})\,\texttt{\char 123\relax}\,\mathit{y}\mathop{\texttt{=>}}\mathit{e_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\,\texttt{\char 125\relax}\,\,\texttt{\char 123\relax}\,\mathit{y}\mathop{\texttt{=>}}\mathit{e_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\,\texttt{\char 125\relax}\,:\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\mathop{|}\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\dashv\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\mathop{|}\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\quad\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={\hypertarget{ottalt:rule:ott:cmd-ty-typetest}{{{cmd-ty-typetest}}}
}{},}\frac{\Gamma(\mathit{x})=\mathit{k}~\mathit{CL}\quad\quad\mathbf{ftype}\,(\mathit{CL},\mathit{f})=\mathit{t}\quad\quad\vdash\mathit{k}\mathop{\odot}\mathit{t}}{\Gamma\vdash*\mathit{x}.\mathit{f}:(\mathit{k}\mathop{\odot}\mathit{t})\dashv\Gamma}\quad\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={\hypertarget{ottalt:rule:ott:cmd-ty-deref-field}{{{cmd-ty-deref-field}}}
}{},}\frac{\Gamma(\mathit{x})=\textsf{var}~\texttt{Cell}[\mathit{t}]\vdash\textsf{var}\mathop{\odot}\mathit{t}}{\Gamma\vdash*\mathit{x}:(\textsf{var}\mathop{\odot}\mathit{t})\dashv\Gamma}\quad\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={\hypertarget{ottalt:rule:ott:cmd-ty-deref-var}{{{cmd-ty-deref-var}}}
}{},}\frac{\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash\mathit{use}:\mathit{t}\dashv\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}(\mathit{x})=\mathit{k}~\mathit{CL}\quad\quad\mathbf{ftype}\,(\mathit{CL},\mathit{f})=\mathit{t}\mathit{k}\in\{\textsf{mut},\textsf{tmp}\}}{\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash\mathit{x}.\mathit{f}:=\mathit{use}:\mathit{t}\dashv\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\quad\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={\hypertarget{ottalt:rule:ott:cmd-ty-assign}{{{cmd-ty-assign}}}
}{},}\frac{\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash\mathit{use}:\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\dashv\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}[\mathit{x}:\textsf{var}~\texttt{Cell}[\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{2}}}}]]}{\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash\mathit{x}:=\mathit{use}:\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\dashv\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}[\mathit{x}:\textsf{var}~\texttt{Cell}[\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}}]]}\quad\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={\hypertarget{ottalt:rule:ott:cmd-ty-assign-var}{{{cmd-ty-assign-var}}}
}{},}\frac{\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash\mathit{use}:\mathit{t}\dashv\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}}{\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash\mathbf{var}\,\mathit{use}:\textsf{var}~\texttt{Cell}[\mathit{t}]\dashv\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\quad\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={\hypertarget{ottalt:rule:ott:cmd-ty-create-var}{{{cmd-ty-create-var}}}
}{},}\frac{\mathbf{fnctype}\,(\mathit{fnc})=(\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\,...\,,\mathit{t_{\mathit{n}}})\rightarrow\mathit{t}\quad\quad\forall\mathit{i}\in[1,\mathit{n}].~\Gamma_{\mathit{i}}\vdash\mathit{use_{\mathit{i}}}:\mathit{t_{\mathit{i}}}\dashv\Gamma_{i+1}}{\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash\mathit{fnc}(\mathit{use_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\,...\,,\mathit{use_{\mathit{n}}}):\mathit{t}\dashv\Gamma_{n+1}}\quad\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={\hypertarget{ottalt:rule:ott:cmd-ty-call}{{{cmd-ty-call}}}

Appendix F
Proofs

F.1.
Theorem: Progress of Command Language

\begin{array}[]{l}\overline{\Gamma}\vdash\texttt{\char 123\relax}de\texttt{\char 125\relax}\implies\\
\quad de=\mathit{use}~\lor~de=\mathbf{Failure}~\lor~\exists\mathit{Eff},de^{\prime}.~de\xrightarrow{\mathit{Eff}}de^{\prime}\\
\end{array}

F.1.1.
Proof Sketch

The proof of progress is very simple since there are very few
rules that have premises that can be false: function calls must
refer to existing functions and objects can only be constructed
with capability
mut
,
tmp
or
iso
. All other rules are
always applicable when the expression has the right shape.

F.1.2.
Proof of Theorem

We begin by inversion of
\overline{\Gamma}\vdash\texttt{\char 123\relax}de\texttt{\char 125\relax}
and get

\overline{\Gamma}\vdash de:t\dashv\Gamma^{\prime}
. We proceed by
induction over the dynamic typing judgment.

Case 1

CMD-DYN-TY-FAILURE
:,
de=\mathbf{Failure}

Proof holds trivially

Case 2

CMD-DYN-TY-EXPR
:,
de=e

A1

\overline{\Gamma}=\Gamma

We proceed by induction over the static typing judgment

\Gamma\vdash de:t\dashv\Gamma^{\prime}
:

Case 2.a

CMD-TY-USE-KEEP
:,
de=x

Proof holds trivially.

Case 2.b

CMD-TY-USE-DROP
:,
de=\texttt{{drop}}~x

Proof holds trivially.

Case 2.c

CMD-TY-TYPETEST
:,
{{de=\texttt{{if}}~\texttt{{typetest}}(\mathit{use},t_{1})\{y\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@language\tt=\textgreater}}}e_{1}\}\{y\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@language\tt=\textgreater}}}e_{2}\}

We pick any
k~CL
.

–

If
k~CL<:t
, the configuration steps by

CMD-IF-TYPETEST-TRUE
.

–

If
k~CL\not<:t
, the
configuration steps by
CMD-IF-TYPETEST-FALSE
.

Case 2.d

CMD-TY-LET
:

,
de=\texttt{{let}}~x=b~\texttt{{in}}~e

By
CMD-TY-LET
we have

A1

\Gamma\vdash b:t^{\prime}\dashv\Gamma^{\prime\prime}

We proceed by cases on the shape of
b
.

–

If

, then by the
inversion lemma with

we get

,

. The configuration steps by
CMD-CALL
.

we get

. The configuration steps by

CMD-LET-EFF

–

If
b=\texttt{{enter}}...
, the configuration steps by

CMD-ENTER-FIELD
,
CMD-ENTER-VAR
or

CMD-ENTER-FAIL

–

For all remaining cases, the configuration steps by

CMD-LET-EFF
.

,

:

A1

\overline{\Gamma}=\overline{\Gamma}^{\prime}::\Gamma

We proceed by cases on whether

of
b

–

Case 2Case 2.d

,
since every non-dynamic typing derivation needs to use

CMD-DYN-TY-EXPR
, which requires an empty
\overline{\Gamma}
.

–

If it is not, we have either
db=de^{\prime}
or

{db=\texttt{{entered}}~y.f~w.\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@language\tt{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{val}}}}}}\texttt{\char 123\relax}de^{\prime}\texttt{\char 125\relax}

F.2.
Theorem: Preservation of Command Language

\begin{array}[]{l}\overline{\Gamma}\vdash\texttt{\char 123\relax}de\texttt{\char 125\relax}~\land~\texttt{\char 123\relax}de\texttt{\char 125\relax}\xrightarrow{\mathit{Eff}}\texttt{\char 123\relax}de^{\prime}\texttt{\char 125\relax}\implies\\
\quad\exists\overline{\Gamma}^{\prime}.~\overline{\Gamma}^{\prime}\vdash\texttt{\char 123\relax}de^{\prime}\texttt{\char 125\relax}~\land~\overline{\Gamma}\vdash\mathit{Eff}\dashv\overline{\Gamma}^{\prime}\end{array}

F.2.1.
Proof Sketch

The proof of preservation ensures that stepping preserves
well-formedness and that it produces a well-formed effect. The
former is straightforward since the only changes we make to the
configuration is alpha conversion of subexpressions, introduction
of (well-formed) function bodies, and introduction of
entered

blocks from (well-formed)
enter
blocks.
The latter comes from lifting the rules for expression typing to
the rules for well-formed effects, via the inversion lemma.

F.2.2.
Proof of Theorem

We state an alpha conversion property, the observation that we can
change the name of a variable without affecting well-formedness of
an expression:

\begin{array}[]{l}\Gamma[x:t_{1}]\vdash e:t\dashv\Gamma^{\prime}[x:t_{2}]~\land~y~\mathit{fresh}\implies\\
\quad\Gamma[y:t_{1}]\vdash e[x\mapsto y]:t\dashv\Gamma^{\prime}[y:t_{2}]\\
\\
\Gamma[x:t_{1}]\vdash e:t\dashv\Gamma^{\prime}[x:\mathbf{undef}]~\land~y~\mathit{fresh}\implies\\
\quad\Gamma[y:t_{1}]\vdash e[x\mapsto y]:t\dashv\Gamma^{\prime}[y:\mathbf{undef}]\\
\end{array}

We also note that the type system supports weakening of the typing
context as long as the bound variables in the expression are not
in the domain of the added context.

\begin{array}[]{l}\Gamma_{1}\vdash e:t\dashv\Gamma_{2}~\land~\vdash\Gamma,\Gamma_{1}~\land~\mathbf{BV}(e)\cap\mathbf{dom}(\Gamma)=\emptyset\implies\\
\quad\Gamma,\Gamma_{1}\vdash e:t\dashv\Gamma,\Gamma_{2}\end{array}

We start the proof of preservation by inversion of well-formedness
of the configuration and get

\overline{\Gamma}\vdash de:t\dashv\Gamma^{\prime}
.
We proceed by induction over the dynamic typing judgment.

Case 1

CMD-DYN-TY-FAILURE
:,
de=\mathbf{Failure}

Proof holds vacuously as the configuration does not step.

Case 2

CMD-DYN-TY-EXPR
:,
de=e

G1

\overline{\Gamma}=\Gamma

We proceed by induction over the static typing judgment

\Gamma\vdash de:t\dashv\Gamma^{\prime}
:

Case 2.a

CMD-TY-USE-KEEP
:,
de=x

Proof holds vacuously as the configuration does not step.

Case 2.b

CMD-TY-USE-DROP
:,
de=\texttt{{drop}}~x

Proof holds vacuously as the configuration does not step.

Case 2.c

CMD-TY-TYPETEST
:,
{{de=\texttt{{if}}~\texttt{{typetest}}(\mathit{use},t^{\prime})\{y\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@language\tt=\textgreater}}}e_{1}\}\{y\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@language\tt=\textgreater}}}e_{2}\}

By
CMD-TY-TYPETEST
we have

A1

\Gamma\vdash\mathit{use}:t^{\prime\prime}\dashv\Gamma^{\prime\prime}

A2

A3

Case 3

F.3.
Theorem: Progress of Region Semantics

\begin{array}[]{l}\vdash\langle\texttt{\char 123\relax}de\texttt{\char 125\relax}~\langle\mathit{RS};H_{op};H_{cl};H_{fr}\rangle\land\texttt{\char 123\relax}de\texttt{\char 125\relax}\xrightarrow{\mathit{Eff}}\texttt{\char 123\relax}de^{\prime}\texttt{\char 125\relax}\implies\\
\quad\exists\mathit{Eff}^{\prime},\texttt{\char 123\relax}de\texttt{\char 125\relax}\xrightarrow{\mathit{Eff}^{\prime}}\texttt{\char 123\relax}de^{\prime}\texttt{\char 125\relax}~\land~\langle\mathit{RS};H_{op};H_{cl};H_{fr}\rangle\xrightarrow{\mathit{Eff}^{\prime}}\langle\mathit{RS}^{\prime};H^{\prime}_{op};H^{\prime}_{cl};H^{\prime}_{fr}\rangle\end{array}

F.3.1.
Proof Sketch

The proof of progress ensures that if the command semantics
produces a well-formed effect, there is an effect that steps both
the command semantics and the region semantics. In practice the
two effects will only be different for dynamic type tests, where
the command semantics always allows both effects
\mathit{cast}

and
\mathit{nocast}
with any type argument, and when

enter
ing a region, where the command semantics always allows
both the effects
\mathit{enter}
and
\mathit{badenter}
. The
region semantics is always able to “select” an effect that works
for both semantics.
The rest of the proof is straightforward and comes down to the
fact that looking up a well-defined name in a well-formed state
always succeeds.

F.3.2.
Proof of Theorem

Whenever we have a well-formed
\mathit{use}
of a variable, the
corresponding
\mathbf{get}
in the region semantics produces a value of
the corresponding type, and looking up that value in the region
stack or heap produces an object of that type. We call this the
well-formed lookup property and it follows from lemmas

F.7
,
F.8
and

F.9
.
We will sometimes use the
\mathsf{capability\_ok}
property to narrow where we are
looking for an object. For example, we know that a
mut

reference points to an open heap region.
Similarly, an update of an existing object via a
\mathsf{store}
,

\mathsf{stack\_store}
or
\mathsf{heap\_store}
always succeeds.
We call this property well-formed stores.

We start the proof by inversion of
TANDEM-WF
and get

\overline{\Gamma}\vdash\texttt{\char 123\relax}de\texttt{\char 125\relax}
. By preservation of the command
language we get
\overline{\Gamma}\vdash\mathit{Eff}\dashv\overline{\Gamma}^{\prime}
.
We proceed by induction over the well-formed effect judgment.

Case 1

WF-EFF-EPS
:,
\mathit{Eff}=\epsilon

The configuration steps trivially by
REGION-EPS
.

Case 2

WF-EFF-LOAD
:,
\mathit{Eff}=\textit{load}(x,y.f)

A1

\overline{\Gamma}=\Gamma::\overline{\Gamma}_{2}

A2

x~\mathit{fresh}

A3

\Gamma(y)=k~CL

A4

\mathbf{ftype}(CL,f)=t

A5

k\neq\textsf{iso}{}

–

By
WF-RS-CONS
with

Case 2A1

we have

\mathit{RS}=(r,S,F)::\mathit{RS}^{\prime}
.

–

By well-formed lookup with

Case 2A3

we get

F(y)=(k,\iota)
.

–

Since only
iso
references can point to closed regions
by
\mathsf{capability\_ok}
, by

Case 2A5

and well-formed lookup we have

{\mathsf{cfg\_load}((r,S,F)::\mathit{RS}^{\prime},H_{op}\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@language\tt\textasteriskcentered}}}H_{fr},\iota)=o[f\mapsto v]
.

–

The configuration steps with
\mathit{Eff}
by
REGION-LOAD
with
\mathit{Eff}
.

Case 3

WF-EFF-SWAP-CLASS
:,
\mathit{Eff}=\textit{swap}(x,y.f,\mathit{use})

A1

\overline{\Gamma}=\Gamma::\overline{\Gamma}_{2}

A2

x~\mathit{fresh}

A3

\Gamma\vdash\mathit{use}:t\dashv\Gamma^{\prime}

A4

\Gamma^{\prime}(y)=k~CL

A5

\mathbf{ftype}(CL,f)=t

A6

–

with

Case 3A1

we have

.

with a well-formed

under

\Gamma^{\prime}
.

.

points into region
r
on the heap
and we have

.

.

.

–

The configuration steps by
REGION-SWAP-HEAP
with
\mathit{Eff}
.

\iota

on the region
stack.

.

Case 4

WF-EFF-SWAP-VAR
:,
{\mathit{Eff}=\textit{swap}(x,y.\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@language\tt{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{val}}}}}},\mathit{use})

Similar to

going into

Case 5

F.4.
Theorem: Preservation of Region Semantics

The region semantics has a property similar to preservation that we express like
this.

\begin{array}[]{l}\overline{\Gamma};\Delta;\Psi\vdash\mathit{rcfg}~\land~\mathsf{capability\_ok}(\mathit{rcfg})~\land~\mathsf{topology\_ok}(\mathit{rcfg})~\land~\mathit{rcfg}\xrightarrow{\mathit{Eff}}\mathit{rcfg}^{\prime}~\land~\overline{\Gamma}\vdash\mathit{Eff}\dashv\overline{\Gamma}^{\prime}\implies\\
\quad\exists\Delta^{\prime},\Psi^{\prime}.\\
\qquad\overline{\Gamma}^{\prime};\Delta^{\prime};\Psi^{\prime}\vdash\mathit{rcfg}^{\prime}~\land~\mathsf{capability\_ok}(\mathit{rcfg}^{\prime})~\land~\mathsf{topology\_ok}(\mathit{rcfg}^{\prime})\end{array}

F.4.1.
Proof Sketch

This is by far the most complex proof for this system. We proceed by induction
on the well-formedness relation for
\mathit{Eff}
. The main problem here is the base
cases, for which we first prove well-typedness and then the invariants
\mathsf{capability\_ok}
and

\mathsf{topology\_ok}
. The proof of well-typedness is done by a standard induction
argument on the well-typedness relations for each part of the configuration. For
the invariants we argue about the restrictions of each reference in the object
graph, with regards to its capability.

Each step of the region configuration will have a corresponding action
(removal/addition of locations and references) on the region graph. Furthemore,
because of well-typedness we can express this action using only the object graph

G(\mathit{rcfg})
, the region ordering
\rho(\overline{\Gamma},\mathit{rcfg})
and
\mathit{Eff}
. We use this
fact and the constraints imposed by the reference capabilities to argue that the
invariants hold in the resulting configuration.

F.4.2.
Proof of Theorem

Main 1

\mathit{rcfg}\xrightarrow{\mathit{Eff}}\mathit{rcfg}^{\prime}

Main 2

\overline{\Gamma}\vdash\mathit{Eff}\dashv\overline{\Gamma}^{\prime}

Main 3

\overline{\Gamma};\Delta;\Psi\vdash\mathit{rcfg}

Main 4

\mathsf{capability\_ok}(\mathit{rcfg})

Main 5

\mathsf{topology\_ok}(\mathit{rcfg})

We proceed by structural induction on the derivation of
\overline{\Gamma}\vdash\mathit{Eff}\dashv\overline{\Gamma}^{\prime}

We make some general

:

If we have

1

If

Then

\mathsf{region\_order}1(r::\rho,\mathit{Cl},\mathit{Fr},\mathit{loc}_{1}[r_{1}]\xrightarrow{f,k}\mathit{loc}_{2}[r_{2}])

Follows
by

and

will be
unaffected.

This in turn implies
