F.4.3.
Observations for Proof

O1

\mathcal{G}\subseteq\mathcal{G}^{\prime}~\land~\mathsf{var\_unique}(\mathcal{G}^{\prime})\implies\mathsf{var\_unique}(\mathcal{G})

O2

If

–

\mathit{ref}=\mathit{loc}_{1}\xrightarrow{f,k}\mathit{loc}_{2}\in\mathcal{G}

–

k\neq\textsf{var}

–

k^{\prime}\neq\textsf{var}

–

\mathsf{var\_unique}(\mathcal{G})

Then

–

\mathsf{var\_unique}(\mathcal{G}+(\_\xrightarrow{f^{\prime},k^{\prime}}\mathit{loc}_{2}))

–

\mathsf{var\_unique}(\mathcal{G}-\mathit{ref}+(\_\xrightarrow{f^{\prime},k^{\prime}}\mathit{loc}_{2}))

Follows
from
k\neq\textsf{var}{}
implying that there is no reference
\_\xrightarrow{g,k^{\prime\prime}}\mathit{loc}_{2}
with
k^{\prime\prime}=\textsf{var}{}
.

O3

\mathsf{var\_unique}(\mathcal{G})~\land~(\mathit{loc}_{1}\xrightarrow{f,\textsf{var}}\mathit{loc}_{2})\in\mathcal{G}\implies\mathsf{var\_unique}(\mathcal{G}-(\mathit{loc}_{1}\xrightarrow{f,\textsf{var}}\mathit{loc}_{2})+(\mathit{loc}_{3}\xrightarrow{f^{\prime},k^{\prime}}\mathit{loc}_{2})

O4

If
\mathit{ref}=(\mathit{loc}_{1}[r_{1}]\xrightarrow{f,k}\mathit{loc}_{2}[r_{2}]
),

\mathit{ref}^{\prime}=(\mathit{loc}_{1}^{\prime}[r_{1}^{\prime}]\xrightarrow{f^{\prime},k^{\prime}}\mathit{loc}_{2}[r_{2}])
,
\mathit{ref}\in\mathcal{G}
,

\rho\vdash r_{2}\leq r_{1}\implies\rho\vdash r_{2}\leq r_{1}^{\prime}
and

\mathsf{topology\_ok\_graph}(\rho,\mathit{Fr},\mathcal{G})

then

\mathsf{topology\_ok\_graph}(\rho,\mathit{Fr},\mathcal{G}-\mathit{ref}+\mathit{ref}^{\prime})

Follows
from a simple case analysis on the clauses of
\mathsf{topology\_pw\_ok}
.

O5

If
\overline{\Gamma}_{1}\mathop{\overset{\mathsf{pw}}{\subseteq}}\overline{\Gamma}_{2}
and

\overline{\Gamma}_{1}\vdash\mathit{rcfg}
, then
\overline{\Gamma}_{1}|\overline{\Gamma}_{2}\vdash\mathit{rcfg}
.

O6

If
\overline{\Gamma}_{1}\mathop{\overset{\mathsf{pw}}{\subseteq}}\overline{\Gamma}_{2}
,

\overline{\Gamma}_{1}\vdash\mathit{Eff}\dashv\overline{\Gamma}_{1}^{\prime}
and
\overline{\Gamma}_{2}\vdash\mathit{Eff}\dashv\overline{\Gamma}_{2}^{\prime}
, then
\overline{\Gamma}_{1}^{\prime}\mathop{\overset{\mathsf{pw}}{\subseteq}}\overline{\Gamma}_{2}^{\prime}
.

Follows
by induction on
\overline{\Gamma}_{1}\vdash\mathit{Eff}\dashv\overline{\Gamma}_{1}^{\prime}
.

O7

If
\overline{\Gamma}[x:t|t^{\prime}]\vdash\mathit{rcfg}
, then

\overline{\Gamma}[x:t]\vdash\mathit{rcfg}
or
\overline{\Gamma}[x:t^{\prime}]\vdash\mathit{rcfg}

Follows
since the actual type of
x
must be a subtype of
t|t^{\prime}
, meaning that
the actual type is a subtype of either
t
or
t^{\prime}
.

O8

If
t<:t^{\prime}
, then
k\mathop{\odot}t<:k\mathop{\odot}t^{\prime}

O9

t<:t^{\prime}
and

\mathsf{cap}(iso,t)
implies that
\textsf{paused}\mathop{\odot}t=~\downarrow~
.
Follows
by
definition of subtyping and view-point adaptation (
4
).

O10

\Delta;\Gamma\vdash F
,
\iota\not\in\mathbf{dom}(\Delta)
then
\Delta,\iota:CL;\Gamma\vdash F
.
Follows
from that

\iota\not\in\mathbf{dom}(\Delta)
implies that
\iota\not\in\mathbf{rng}(F)

O11

If
t<:t^{\prime}
then
\mathit{make\_mut}(()t)<:\mathit{make\_mut}(()t^{\prime})
.
Follows
by induction on structure of
t
.

O12

If

–

\mathsf{entrypoints\_ok}(\rho,\mathcal{G})

–

\mathsf{topology\_ok\_graph}(\rho,\mathit{Fr},\mathcal{G})

–

\rho
contains unique region ids

–

\rho=\dots\underset{x.f}{::}r^{\prime}\dots\underset{y.g}{::}r^{\prime\prime}::\dots\varepsilon

Then

–

\mathit{ref}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathtt{root}(r^{\prime}),x)=\mathtt{root}(r^{\prime})\xrightarrow{x,k_{x}}\mathit{loc}_{x}

–

\mathit{ref}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathtt{root}(r^{\prime\prime}),y)=\mathtt{root}(r^{\prime\prime})\xrightarrow{y,k_{y}}\mathit{loc}_{y}

–

Either
\mathit{loc}_{x}\neq\mathit{loc}_{y}
or
f\neq g
.

Follows
from uniqueness constraints of
\mathsf{topology\_ok\_graph}
and that
\rho
does not
have repeated region ids.

O13

If

\mathsf{region\_order}1(r::\rho,\mathit{Cl},\mathit{Fr},(\mathtt{root}(r)\xrightarrow{x,\textsf{iso}{}}\mathit{loc}_{x}[r^{\prime}]))
,
then
r^{\prime}\in\mathit{Cl}
.

Follows
by definition of
\mathsf{region\_order}1

O14

If

–

\rho=r::\rho^{*}

–

\vdash\rho,\mathit{Cl},\mathit{Fr}

–

\mathsf{var\_unique}(\mathcal{G})

–

\mathsf{region\_order}(\rho,\mathit{Cl},\mathit{Fr},\mathcal{G})

–

\mathsf{location\_ok}(\mathcal{G})

–

\mathsf{deep\_freeze}(\mathit{Fr},\mathcal{G})

–

\mathsf{topology\_ok\_graph}(\rho,\mathit{Fr},\mathcal{G})

–

\mathsf{entrypoints\_ok}(\rho,\mathcal{G})

–

\mathtt{root}(r)\xrightarrow{x,k_{x}}\mathit{loc}_{x}[r_{x}]\in\mathcal{G}

–

\mathit{loc}_{x}[r_{x}]\xrightarrow{f,k_{f}}\mathit{loc}_{f}\in\mathcal{G}

–

r_{x}\in\rho

–

\mathit{ref}=\mathtt{root}(r)\xrightarrow{z,k_{x}\mathop{\odot}k_{f}}\mathit{loc}_{f}

Then

–

\mathsf{var\_unique}(\mathcal{G}+\mathit{ref})

–

\mathsf{region\_order}(\rho,\mathit{Cl},\mathit{Fr},\mathcal{G}+\mathit{ref})

–

\mathsf{location\_ok}(\mathcal{G}+\mathit{ref})

–

\mathsf{deep\_freeze}(\mathit{Fr},\mathcal{G}+\mathit{ref})

–

\mathsf{topology\_ok\_graph}(\rho,\mathit{Fr},\mathcal{G}+\mathit{ref})

–

\mathsf{entrypoints\_ok}(\rho,\mathcal{G}+\mathit{ref})

Follows
from a case analysis on
k_{x},k_{f}
.

F.5.
Theorem: Soundness of the Tandem Semantics

A well formed configuration is either done, is has reached a failed state
(because of failing to enter an already opened region), or can take a step to a
well formed configuration.

\displaystyle\vdash\langle\texttt{\char 123\relax}de\texttt{\char 125\relax}~\mathit{rcfg}\rangle\implies de=\mathit{use}~\lor~de=\mathbf{Failure}~\lor~\langle\texttt{\char 123\relax}de\texttt{\char 125\relax}~\mathit{rcfg}\rangle\rightarrow\langle\texttt{\char 123\relax}de^{\prime}\texttt{\char 125\relax}~\mathit{rcfg}^{\prime}\rangle~\land~\vdash\langle\texttt{\char 123\relax}de^{\prime}\texttt{\char 125\relax}~\mathit{rcfg}^{\prime}\rangle

F.5.1.
Proof of Theorem

Follows from theorems
F.1
,
F.2
,

F.3
, and
F.4
.

F.6.
Lemma: Subtyping is Transitive

Subtyping is transitive, i.e. if
t_{1}<:t_{2}
and
t_{2}<:t_{3}
, then
t_{1}<:t_{3}
.

F.6.1.
Proof of Lemma

By strong induction over the subtyping relations.

F.7.
Lemma: get is Total

\begin{array}[]{l}\Delta;\Gamma\vdash F~\land~\Gamma\vdash use:t\dashv\Gamma^{\prime}\implies\\
\quad\exists F^{\prime},k,\iota.\mathbf{get}(F,use)=((k,\iota),F^{\prime})\end{array}

F.7.1.
Proof of Lemma

By induction over the well-formedness judgment of
F
.

F.8.
Lemma: Well-Formedness of
get

\displaystyle\forall

\displaystyle\Delta,\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime},use,F,F^{\prime},k,\iota.

\displaystyle\Delta;\Gamma\vdash F~\land~

\displaystyle\Gamma\vdash use:t\dashv\Gamma^{\prime}~\land~

\displaystyle\mathbf{get}(F,use)=((k,\iota),F^{\prime})

\displaystyle\implies

\displaystyle\Delta;\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash F^{\prime}~\land~

\displaystyle\iota:CL\in\Delta~\land~

\displaystyle k~CL<:t

F.8.1.
Observations

It is a simple exercise to prove the following observations

O1

If
\Delta;\Gamma\vdash F
, then if

\phi
is a permutation of the order of the mappings in
\Gamma
and
F
,
we have
\Delta;\phi(\Gamma)\vdash\phi(F)
.

O2

If
t<:t^{\prime}
, then
t[k/k^{\prime}]<:t^{\prime}[k/k^{\prime}]
for all
capabilities
k,k^{\prime}
.

F.8.2.
Proof of Lemma

In order to prove the lemma we begin by stating the assumptions

Main 1

\Delta;\Gamma\vdash F

Main 2

\Gamma\vdash\mathit{use}:t\dashv\Gamma^{\prime}

Main 3

\mathbf{get}(F,\mathit{use})=((k,\iota),F^{\prime})
.

By structural induction on
\Delta;\Gamma\vdash F
:

Case 1

WF-F-NIL
::
Impossible.

Case 2

WF-F-CONS
::

A1

\Gamma=\Gamma_{1},y:t_{y}

A2

F=F_{1},y\mapsto(k_{y},\iota_{y})

A3

\Delta;\Gamma_{1}\vdash F_{1}

A4

\iota_{y}:~\mathit{CL}_{y}\in\Delta

A5

k_{y}~\mathit{CL}_{y}<:t_{y}

A6

y\not\in\mathbf{dom}(F_{1})

We now split on cases of
\mathit{use}
.

Case 2.1

\mathit{use}=z=y
:

By lemma
F.12
we we have

B1

\mathsf{cap}(\{\textsf{mut},\textsf{tmp},\textsf{paused},\textsf{imm}\},t_{y})

B2

\Gamma^{\prime}=\Gamma_{1},y:t_{y}

B3

t_{y}<:t

By definition of
\mathbf{get}

(iv)

\mathbf{get}(y,(F_{1},y\mapsto(k_{y},\iota_{y})))=((k_{y},\iota_{y}),(F_{1},y\mapsto(k_{y},\iota_{y})))

We can thus identify the following

(v)

k=k_{y}

(vi)

\iota=\iota_{y}

(vii)

~\mathit{CL}=~\mathit{CL}_{y}

(viii)

F^{\prime}=F_{1},y\mapsto(k_{y},\iota_{y})

Together with these facts, and with observations

O1
,
O2
, and lemma

F.6
, it follows that

-

\Delta;\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash F^{\prime}

-

\iota:~\mathit{CL}\in\Delta
, and

-

k~\mathit{CL}<:t
.

Case 2.2

\mathit{use}=z\neq y
:

Follows from definition of
\mathbf{get}
and application of induction hypothesis
together with observation
O1
.

Case 2.3

\mathit{use}=\texttt{{drop}}~z
,
z=y
:

This case is similar to

Case 2Case 2.1

above .

Case 2.4

\mathit{use}=\texttt{{drop}}~z
,
z\neq y
:

Similar to

Case 2Case 2.2

.

Case 3

WF-F-CONS-UNDEF
:

We have the assumptions

A1

\Gamma=\Gamma_{1},y:\mathbf{undef}

A2

F=F_{1},y\mapsto\mathbf{undef}

A3

\Delta;\Gamma_{1}\vdash F_{1}

A4

\iota_{y}:~\mathit{CL}_{y}\in\Delta

A5

k_{y}~\mathit{CL}_{y}<:t_{y}

A6

y\not\in\mathbf{dom}(F_{1})

Because
y
is undefined in both
\Gamma
and
F
, looking at the definition
of
\mathbf{get}
together with

Main 2

, the only possible cases
are
\mathit{use}=\texttt{{drop}}~z
or
\mathit{use}=z
, where
z\neq y
. We can thus apply
the induction hypothesis similar to

Case 2Case 2.2

above.

F.9.
Lemma: Well-Formed cfg_load

\begin{array}[]{l}\mathit{rcfg}=\langle RS;H_{op};H_{cl};H_{fr}\rangle~\land~\overline{\Gamma};\Delta;\Psi\vdash\mathit{rcfg}~\land~\iota:CL\in\Delta\implies\\
\quad\mathsf{cfg\_load}(RS,H_{op}*H_{cl}*H_{fr})=(CL,F)~\land~\Delta;\mathbf{ftypes}(CL)\vdash F\end{array}

F.9.1.
Proof of Lemma

By induction over the well-formedness rules for
\mathit{RS}
and
H
.
Note that
every

\iota
in
\Delta
maps to
exactly
one
object in the configuration.

F.10.
Lemma: Well-Formed Environment

If
\Gamma\vdash b:t\dashv\Gamma^{\prime}
, then
\vdash\Gamma
and
\vdash\Gamma^{\prime}
.

F.10.1.
Proof of lemma

By structural induction on
\Gamma\vdash b:t\dashv\Gamma^{\prime}
.

F.11.
Lemma: Well-Formed Type Under Environment

If
\vdash\Gamma
and
\Gamma(x)=t
, then
\vdash t
.

F.11.1.
Proof of lemma

By structural induction on
\vdash\Gamma
.

F.12.
Inversion Lemma

Because of subtyping, we cannot invert typing judgments directly
to get useful information. Instead we prove an inversion lemma
that gives us the premises of the corresponding rule, with
subtyping information regarding the result type.

Because some of the type rules are only defined when variables
have types of the shape
k~CL
, we define a helper function for
calculating the type of a field lookup for type unions (this is
the result of typing
*
x
.
f
when
x
has type
t
):

\mathbf{fresult}(t,f)=\begin{cases}k\mathop{\odot}\mathbf{ftype}(CL,f)&\text{if }t=k~CL\\
\mathbf{fresult}(t_{1},f)~|~\mathbf{fresult}(t_{2},f)&\text{if }t=t_{1}~|~t_{2}\end{cases}

When doing strong updates of ref cells, we need to turn a union of
types into a union of
Cell
types:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\mathit{make\_cell}(k~CL)&=&\textsf{var}{}~\texttt{Cell}[k~CL]\\
\mathit{make\_cell}(t_{1}~|~t_{2})&=&\mathit{make\_cell}(t_{1})~|~\mathit{make\_cell}(t_{2})\end{array}

When freezing a type, we need to change
iso
capabilities to
imm
:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\mathit{make\_imm}(k~CL)&=&\begin{cases}\textsf{imm}~CL&\text{if }k=\textsf{iso}\\
k~CL&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}\\
\mathit{make\_imm}(t_{1}~|~t_{2})&=&\mathit{make\_imm}(t_{1})~|~\mathit{make\_imm}(t_{2})\end{array}

The inversion lemma itself is stated as follows:

{{{{{{\begin{array}[]{rcl}\Gamma\vdash x:t\dashv\Gamma^{\prime}&\iff&\vdash\Gamma~\land~\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}[x:t^{\prime}]~\land~t^{\prime}<:t~\land~\mathsf{cap}(\{\textsf{iso},\textsf{var}\}^{c},t^{\prime})~\land\\
&&\Gamma^{\prime}=\Gamma_{1}[x:t^{\prime}]\\
\\
\Gamma\vdash\texttt{{drop}}~x:t\dashv\Gamma^{\prime}&\iff&\vdash\Gamma~\land~\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}[x:t^{\prime}]~\land~t^{\prime}<:t~\land~\Gamma^{\prime}=\Gamma_{1}[x:\mathbf{undef}]\\
\\
\Gamma\vdash\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@language\tt\textasteriskcentered}}}x.f:t\dashv\Gamma^{\prime}&\implies&\Gamma(x)=t_{x}~\land~\mathbf{fresult}(t_{x},f)=t^{\prime}~\land~t^{\prime}<:t\\
&&\mathsf{cap}(\{\textsf{iso}\}^{c},t_{x})~\land~\forall k~CL\in t_{x}.\vdash k\mathop{\odot}t^{\prime}\\
\\
\Gamma\vdash\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@language\tt\textasteriskcentered}}}x:t\dashv\Gamma^{\prime}&\implies&\Gamma(x)=t_{x}~\land~\mathbf{fresult}(t_{x},\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@language\tt{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{val}}}}}})=t^{\prime}~\land~t^{\prime}<:t\\
&&\mathsf{cap}(\textsf{var}{},t_{x})~\land~\vdash\textsf{var}{}\mathop{\odot}t^{\prime}\\
\\
\Gamma\vdash x.f~\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@language\tt:=}}}~\mathit{use}:t\dashv\Gamma^{\prime}&\implies&\Gamma\vdash\mathit{use}:t^{\prime}\dashv\Gamma^{\prime}~\land~\Gamma^{\prime}(x)=t_{x}~\land\\
&&\mathsf{cap}(\{\textsf{mut}{},\textsf{tmp}\},t_{x})~\land~\mathbf{fresult}(t_{x},f)=t^{\prime}~\land~t^{\prime}<:t\\
\\
\Gamma\vdash x\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@language\tt:=}}}~\mathit{use}:t\dashv\Gamma^{\prime}&\implies&\Gamma\vdash\mathit{use}:t^{\prime}\dashv\Gamma^{\prime\prime}[x:t_{x}]~\land~\mathsf{cap}(\textsf{var}{},t_{x})~\land\\
&&\mathbf{fresult}(t_{x},\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@language\tt{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{val}}}}}})=t_{f}~\land~t_{f}<:t~\land\\
&&\Gamma^{\prime}=\Gamma^{\prime\prime}[x:\mathit{make\_cell}[t^{\prime}]]\\
\\
\Gamma_{1}\vdash\mathit{fnc}(\mathit{use}_{1},...,\mathit{use}_{n}):t\dashv\Gamma_{n+1}&\implies&\mathbf{fnctype}(\mathit{fnc})=t_{1},...,t_{n}\rightarrow t^{\prime}~\land~t^{\prime}<:t~\land\\
&&\forall i\in[1,n].\Gamma_{i}\vdash\mathit{use}_{i}:t_{i}\dashv\Gamma_{i+1}\\
\\
\Gamma\vdash\texttt{{var}}~\mathit{use}:t\dashv\Gamma^{\prime}&\implies&\Gamma\vdash\mathit{use}:t^{\prime}\dashv\Gamma^{\prime}~\land~\textsf{var}{}~\texttt{Cell}[t^{\prime}]<:t\\
\\
\Gamma_{1}\vdash\texttt{{new}}~k~C(\overline{\mathit{use}}):t\dashv\Gamma_{n+1}&\implies&\vdash\Gamma_{1}~\land~\vdash k~C~\land~k~C<:t~\land~k\in\{\textsf{mut},\textsf{tmp},\textsf{iso}\}~\land\\
&&\mathbf{ftypes}(C)=f_{1}:t_{1},...f_{n}:t_{n}~\land~\overline{\mathit{use}}=\mathit{use}_{1},...,\mathit{use}_{n}~\land\\
&&\forall i\in[1,n].(\Gamma_{i}\vdash\mathit{use}_{i}:t^{\prime}_{i}\dashv\Gamma_{i+1}~\land~t^{\prime}_{i}<:t_{i})\\
&&\quad k=\textsf{iso}\implies\mathsf{cap}(\{\textsf{iso},\textsf{imm}\},t^{\prime}_{i})\\
\\
\Gamma\vdash\texttt{{freeze}}~\mathit{use}:t\dashv\Gamma^{\prime}&\implies&\Gamma\vdash\mathit{use}:t^{\prime}\dashv\Gamma^{\prime}~\land~\mathsf{cap}(iso,t^{\prime})~\land~\mathit{make\_imm}(t^{\prime})<:t)\\
\\
\Gamma\vdash\texttt{{merge}}~\mathit{use}:t\dashv\Gamma^{\prime}&\implies&\Gamma\vdash\mathit{use}:t^{\prime}\dashv\Gamma^{\prime}~\land~\mathsf{cap}(iso,t^{\prime})~\land~\mathit{make\_mut}(t^{\prime})<:t\\
\\
\text{Continued on next page...}\end{array}

{{{\begin{array}[]{l}\Gamma_{1}\vdash\texttt{{enter}}~x.f~[\overline{y=\mathit{use}}]\texttt{\char 123\relax}z~\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@language\tt=\textgreater}}}~e\texttt{\char 125\relax}:t\dashv\Gamma_{n+1}\implies\\
\quad\forall i\in[1,n].\Gamma_{i}\vdash\mathit{use}_{i}:t_{i}\dashv\Gamma_{i+1}~\land~\Gamma_{n+1}(x)=t_{x}~\land\\
\quad\mathsf{cap}(\{\textsf{mut},\textsf{tmp},\textsf{var},\textsf{paused}\},t_{x})~\land\\
\quad\mathbf{fresult}(t_{x},f))=t_{f}~\land~\mathsf{cap}(\textsf{iso},t_{f})~\land\\
\quad\Gamma=y_{1}:t^{\prime}_{1},...,y_{n}:t^{\prime}_{n}\\
\quad\quad\text{ where }t^{\prime}_{i}=\begin{cases}t_{i}&\text{if }\mathsf{cap}(\textsf{iso},t_{i})\\
\textsf{paused}\mathop{\odot}t_{i}&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}~\land\\
\quad t_{f}^{\prime}=\mathit{make\_mut}(t_{f})~\land~\mathsf{cap}(\{\textsf{iso},\textsf{imm}\},t^{\prime})~\land~t^{\prime}<:t~\land\\
\quad\Gamma,z:\textsf{tmp}~\texttt{Cell}[t_{f}^{\prime}]\vdash e:t^{\prime}\dashv\Gamma^{\prime},z:\textsf{tmp}~\texttt{Cell}[t_{f}^{\prime}]\\
\\
\Gamma_{1}\vdash\texttt{{enter}}~x~[\overline{y=\mathit{use}}]\texttt{\char 123\relax}z~\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@language\tt=\textgreater}}}~e\texttt{\char 125\relax}:t\dashv\Gamma^{\prime}\implies\\
\quad\forall i\in[1,n].\Gamma_{i}\vdash\mathit{use}_{i}:t_{i}\dashv\Gamma_{i+1}~\land~\Gamma[x:t_{x}]=\Gamma_{n+1}~\land\\
\quad\mathsf{cap}(\textsf{var},t_{x})~\land\\
\quad\mathbf{fresult}(t_{x},\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@language\tt{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{val}}}}}}))=t_{f}~\land~\mathsf{cap}(\textsf{iso},t_{f})~\land\\
\quad\Gamma_{y}=y_{1}:t^{\prime}_{1},...,y_{n}:t^{\prime}_{n}\\
\quad\quad\text{ where }t^{\prime}_{i}=\begin{cases}t_{i}&\text{if }\mathsf{cap}(\textsf{iso},t_{i})\\
\textsf{paused}\mathop{\odot}t_{i}&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}~\land\\
\quad t_{z}=\mathit{make\_cell}(\mathit{make\_mut}(t_{f}))~\land\\
\quad t_{z}^{\prime}=\mathit{make\_cell}(t_{f}^{\prime})~\land\\
\quad\Gamma_{y},z:t_{z}\vdash e:t^{\prime}\dashv\Gamma_{y}^{\prime},z:t_{z}^{\prime}~\land~\mathsf{cap}(\{\textsf{iso},\textsf{imm}\},t^{\prime})~\land\\
\quad\mathsf{cap}(\textsf{mut},t_{f}^{\prime})~\land~t^{\prime}<:t~\land\\
\quad\Gamma^{\prime}=\Gamma[x:t_{x}^{\prime}]~\land~t_{x}^{\prime}=\mathit{make\_cell}(\mathit{make\_iso}(t_{f}^{\prime}))\\
\end{array}

{{\begin{array}[]{l}\overline{\Gamma}\vdash\texttt{{entered}}~x.f~y.\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@language\tt{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{val}}}}}}\texttt{\char 123\relax}de\texttt{\char 125\relax}:t\vdash\Gamma^{\prime}\implies\\
\quad\overline{\Gamma}=\overline{\Gamma}^{\prime}::\Gamma_{1}\underset{x.f}{::}\Gamma_{0}[x:t_{x}]~\land\\
\quad\mathsf{cap}(\{\textsf{mut},\textsf{tmp},\textsf{var},\textsf{paused}\},t_{x})~\land~\mathbf{fresult}(t_{x},f)=t_{f}~\land\\
\quad\mathsf{cap}(\textsf{iso}{},t)~\land~\overline{\Gamma}^{\prime}::\Gamma_{1}\vdash de:t^{\prime}\dashv\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}~\land~\mathsf{cap}(\{\textsf{iso}{},\textsf{imm}{}\},t^{\prime})~\land\\
\quad t^{\prime}<:t~\land~\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}(y)=t_{y}~\land~\mathbf{fresult}(t_{y},\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@language\tt{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{val}}}}}})=t^{\prime\prime}~\land\\
\quad\mathsf{cap}(\textsf{mut}{},t^{\prime\prime})~\land~\mathsf{cap}(\{\textsf{tmp}{},\textsf{var}{}\},t_{y})~\land~\Gamma^{\prime}=\Gamma_{0}[x:t_{x}^{\prime}]~\land\\
\quad t_{x}^{\prime}=\begin{cases}\mathit{make\_cell}(\mathit{make\_iso}(t^{\prime\prime}))&\text{if }t_{x}=\mathit{make\_cell}(\_)\\
t_{x}&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}\\
\end{array}

F.12.1.
Proof of Lemma

We first prove the
\Rightarrow
-cases by induction over the
typing judgments. The only two interesting subcases are using the
rules
CMD-TY-SUB
and
CMD-TY-SPLIT
, which are similar for
all the cases. We show the cases for
x
,
\texttt{{drop}}~x
and
function call here; the remaining cases are similar.

Case 1

\Gamma\vdash x:t\dashv\Gamma^{\prime}
:

Case 1.a

CMD-TY-USE-KEEP
:

–

Proof follows immediately from reflexivity of subtyping

Case 1.b

CMD-TY-SUB
:

A1

\Gamma\vdash x:t^{\prime}\dashv\Gamma^{\prime}

A2

t^{\prime}<:t

–

By the induction hypothesis, we have
\Gamma(x)=t^{\prime\prime}

and
t^{\prime\prime}<:t^{\prime}
,

–

By transitivity of subtyping, we get
t^{\prime\prime}<:t
.

–

The remaining obligations follow from the induction
hypothesis.

Case 1.c

CMD-TY-SPLIT
:

A1

t=t_{x}|t^{\prime}_{x}

A2

\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}[y:t_{y1}~|~t_{y2}]

A3

\Gamma_{1}[y:t_{y1}]\vdash x:t_{x}\dashv\Gamma_{2}

A4

\Gamma_{1}[y:t_{y2}]\vdash x:t^{\prime}_{x}\dashv\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}

A5

\Gamma^{\prime}=\Gamma_{2}~|~\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}

–

if
x\neq y
the proof follows from the induction
hypothesis.

–

If
x=y
, by the induction hypothesis we have

t_{y1}<:t_{x}
and

t_{y2}<:t^{\prime}_{x}
, that
iso
is not
in the capabilities of
t_{y1}
or
t_{y2}
, and that

\Gamma_{2}(x)=t_{y1}
and

\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}(x)=t_{y2}
.

–

From the definition of
\Gamma_{2}~|~\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}
, we get
that
\Gamma^{\prime}(x)=t_{y1}~|~t_{y2}
.

–

From the subtyping rules, we get that

t_{y1}~|~t_{y2}<:t_{x}~|~t^{\prime}_{x}
.

Case 2

\Gamma\vdash\texttt{{drop}}~x:t\dashv\Gamma^{\prime}
:

Case 2.a

CMD-TY-DROP
:

–

Proof follows immediately

Case 2.b

CMD-TY-SUB
:

A1

\Gamma\vdash\texttt{{drop}}~x:t^{\prime}\dashv\Gamma^{\prime}

A2

t^{\prime}<:t

–

By the induction hypothesis, we have
\Gamma(x)=t^{\prime\prime}

and
t^{\prime\prime}<:t^{\prime}
,

–

By transitivity of subtyping, we get
t^{\prime\prime}<:t
.

–

The remaining obligations follow from the induction
hypothesis.

Case 2.c

CMD-TY-SPLIT
:

A1

t=t_{x}|t^{\prime}_{x}

A2

\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}[y:t_{y1}~|~t_{y2}]

A3

\Gamma_{1}[y:t_{y1}]\vdash\texttt{{drop}}~x:t_{x}\dashv\Gamma_{2}

A4

\Gamma_{1}[y:t_{y2}]\vdash\texttt{{drop}}~x:t^{\prime}_{x}\dashv\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}

A5

\Gamma^{\prime}=\Gamma_{2}~|~\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}

–

if
x\neq y
the proof follows from the induction
hypothesis.

–

If
x=y
, by the induction hypothesis we have

t_{y1}<:t_{x}
and
t_{y2}<:t^{\prime}_{x}
, and that

\Gamma_{2}(x)=\mathbf{undef}
and

\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}(x)=\mathbf{undef}
.

–

From the definition of
\Gamma_{2}~|~\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}
, we get
that
\Gamma^{\prime}(x)=\mathbf{undef}
.

–

From the subtyping rules, we get that

t_{y1}~|~t_{y2}<:t_{x}~|~t^{\prime}_{x}
.

Case 3

\Gamma\vdash\mathit{fnc}(use_{1},...,use_{n}):t\dashv\Gamma^{\prime}
:

Case 3.a

CMD-TY-CALL
:

–

Proof follows immediately from reflexivity of subtyping

Case 3.b

CMD-TY-SUB
:

A1

\Gamma\vdash b:t^{\prime}\dashv\Gamma^{\prime}

A2

t^{\prime}<:t

–

By the induction hypothesis, we have

\mathbf{fnctype}(\mathit{fnc})=t_{1},...,t_{n}\rightarrow t^{\prime\prime}
and
t^{\prime\prime}<:t^{\prime}
.

–

By transitivity of subtyping, we get

t^{\prime\prime}<:t
.

–

The rest of the obligations follow from the induction
hypothesis.

Case 3.c

CMD-TY-SPLIT
:

A1

t=t_{b1}|t_{b1}^{\prime}

A2

\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}[x:t_{x1}~|~t_{x2}]

A3

\Gamma_{1}[x:t_{x1}]\vdash b:t_{b1}\dashv\Gamma_{2}

A4

\Gamma_{1}[x:t_{x2}]\vdash b:t_{b1}^{\prime}\dashv\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}

A5

\Gamma^{\prime}=\Gamma_{2}~|~\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}

–

By the induction hypothesis, we have

\mathbf{fnctype}(\mathit{fnc})=t_{1},...,t_{n}\rightarrow t^{\prime}
, and
t^{\prime}<:t_{b1}
and
t^{\prime}<:t_{b2}
.

–

By the subtyping rules,
t^{\prime}<:t_{b1}|t_{b1}^{\prime}
.

–

The arguments are typed using the same reasoning as in the
cases for
b=use
, but generalized to a sequence. We note
that a
drop
of the same variable cannot appear twice in
the same argument list.

We then prove the
\Leftarrow
-cases of
x
and
\texttt{{drop}}~x
by
induction on the shape of
t^{\prime}
.

Case 1

x
:

Case 1.a

t^{\prime}=k~CL
:

\Gamma_{1}[x:k~CL]\vdash x:t\dashv\Gamma_{1}[x:k~CL]
by

CMD-TY-SUB
with
CMD-TY-USE-KEEP
.

Case 1.b

t^{\prime}=t^{\prime}_{1}~|~t^{\prime}_{2}
:

–

By the induction hypothesis, we have

\Gamma_{1}[x:t^{\prime}_{1}]\vdash x:t\dashv\Gamma_{1}[x:t^{\prime}_{1}]

and

\Gamma_{1}[x:t^{\prime}_{2}]\vdash x:t\dashv\Gamma_{1}[x:t^{\prime}_{2}]
.

–

By
CMD-TY-SPLIT
we have

\Gamma_{1}[x:t^{\prime}_{1}~|~t^{\prime}_{2}]\vdash x:t~|~t\dashv\Gamma_{1}[x:t^{\prime}_{1}~|~t^{\prime}_{2}]
.

–

Since
t~|~t<:t
, by
CMD-TY-SUB
we have

\Gamma_{1}[x:t^{\prime}_{1}~|~t^{\prime}_{2}]\vdash x:t\dashv\Gamma_{1}[x:t^{\prime}_{1}~|~t^{\prime}_{2}]
.

Case 2

\texttt{{drop}}~x
:

No induction is needed. We have

\Gamma[x:t^{\prime}]\vdash x:t\dashv\Gamma^{\prime}[x:\mathbf{undef}]
by

CMD-TY-SUB
with
CMD-TY-USE-DROP
.

F.13.
Graph Actions

To describe the action of an effect
\mathit{Eff}
on the object graph
\mathcal{G}
, we
make some basic definitions of what an
graph action
is. The main idea is
that each effect has some basic action consisting of removal and addition of
locations and references.

Given a configuration
\overline{\Gamma}\vdash\mathit{rcfg}
and
\overline{\Gamma}\vdash\mathit{Eff}\dashv\overline{\Gamma}^{\prime}
, the graph
G(\mathit{rcfg})
is well defined and well formed. By progress,
WLOG we can assume that
\mathit{rcfg}\xrightarrow{\mathit{Eff}}\mathit{rcfg}^{\prime}
. For such an
\mathit{Eff}
we can
calculate a delta
\delta
such that
G(\mathit{rcfg}^{\prime})=G(\mathit{rcfg})+\delta
(this
addition is defined below).
\delta
, called a graph action, is a pair of set
actions, one for locations and one for references. A set action is a pair
of sets, one for removal and one for addition:

\begin{array}[]{lcll}\delta&\in&(\delta_{\mathit{LOCS}},\delta_{\mathit{REFS}})&\text{Graph
action:
action for
locations/references}\\
\delta_{E}&\in&\mathcal{P}(E)\times\mathcal{P}(E)&\text{Set action: pair of
\emph{remove} and \emph{add} set}\end{array}

For convenience we define

\varepsilon=(\emptyset,\emptyset).

Addition between a set
E
and a
\delta=(E_{\mathsf{r}},E_{\mathsf{a}})
is
defined as

\begin{array}[]{lcl}E+(E_{\mathsf{r}},E_{\mathsf{a}})&=&(E\setminus E_{\mathsf{r}})\uplus E_{\mathsf{a}}\end{array}

Given a graph action
\delta=(\delta_{l},\delta_{r})
and a graph
\mathcal{G}=(\mathcal{L},\mathcal{R})
, we define

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{G}+\delta&=&(\mathcal{L}+\delta_{l},\mathcal{R}+\delta_{r})\\
\end{array}

Furthermore for convenience, we define the addition of two actions
delta_{1}=(E^{\mathsf{r}}_{1},E^{\mathsf{a}}_{1})
and
\delta_{2}=(E^{\mathsf{r}}_{2},E^{\mathsf{a}}_{2})
as

\delta_{1}+\delta_{2}=(E^{\mathsf{r}}_{1}\uplus E^{\mathsf{r}}_{2},E^{\mathsf{a}}_{1}\uplus E^{\mathsf{a}}_{2})

When not ambiguous, we can write

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{G}+\mathcal{L}&\equiv&\mathcal{G}+((\emptyset,\mathcal{L}),\varepsilon)\\
\mathcal{G}-\mathcal{L}&\equiv&\mathcal{G}+((\mathcal{L},\emptyset),\varepsilon)\\
\mathcal{G}+\mathit{loc}&\equiv&\mathcal{G}+((\emptyset,\{\mathit{loc}\}),\varepsilon)\\
\mathcal{G}-\mathit{loc}&\equiv&\mathcal{G}+((\{\mathit{loc}\},\emptyset),\varepsilon)\\
\mathcal{G}+\mathcal{R}&\equiv&\mathcal{G}+(\varepsilon,(\emptyset,\mathcal{R}))\\
\mathcal{G}-\mathcal{R}&\equiv&\mathcal{G}+(\varepsilon,(\mathcal{R},\emptyset))\\
\mathcal{G}+\mathit{ref}&\equiv&\mathcal{G}+(\varepsilon,(\emptyset,\{\mathit{ref}\}))\\
\mathcal{G}-\mathit{ref}&\equiv&\mathcal{G}+(\varepsilon,(\{\mathit{ref}\},\emptyset))\end{array}

Both
+
and
-
associate to the left.

F.14.
Lemma: Well-Defined Graph Actions

If

–

\overline{\Gamma}\vdash\mathit{rcfg}

–

\overline{\Gamma}\vdash\mathit{Eff}\dashv\overline{\Gamma}^{\prime}

–

\mathit{rcfg}\xrightarrow{\mathit{Eff}}\mathit{rcfg}^{\prime}

then there is a unique
\delta
, such that

G(\mathit{rcfg}^{\prime})=G(\mathit{rcfg})+\delta

Furthermore, this
\delta
can be computed using only
G(\mathit{rcfg}),\rho(\overline{\Gamma},\mathit{rcfg}),\mathit{Eff}
, and access to any fresh
\iota
and
r
used in the step.

F.14.1.
Proof of Lemma

We prove this by defining a partial function, taking a
\rho=\rho(\overline{\Gamma},\mathit{rcfg})
,

\mathcal{G}=G(\mathit{rcfg})
, and
\mathit{Eff}
:

\delta^{*}(\rho,\mathcal{G},\mathit{Eff})=(\delta_{LOC},\delta_{REF})

In the cases below, we make statements of the form

\mathit{ref}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathit{loc},f)=(\mathit{loc}\xrightarrow{f,k_{f}}\mathit{loc}_{f})

These equalities are well-defined because of the well formedness of
\mathit{rcfg}
and

\mathit{Eff}
. Furthermore, uniqueness of
\delta
follows from that
\mathit{ref}_{\mathcal{G}}
is a
partial function.

Case 1

\textit{enter}(w,k,y.f,\overline{x=\mathit{use}})
:

\delta^{*}(r::\rho,\mathcal{G},\textit{enter}(w,k,y.f,\overline{z=\mathit{use}}))=(\delta_{LOC},\delta_{REF})

Looking at the rule
REGION-ENTER-OK
, the
\textit{enter}()
effect
adds two new locations: a new
\mathtt{root}(r^{\prime})
for the region
r^{\prime}
on the region
stack, and a
\mathtt{temp}(r^{\prime},\iota^{\prime\prime})
, representing the
Cell
holding the entry
(with
\iota^{\prime\prime}
a fresh object id). We do not remove any graph locations. I.e.

\delta_{LOC}=(\{\},\{\mathtt{root}(r^{\prime}),\mathtt{temp}(r^{\prime},\iota^{\prime\prime})\}).

As for the references, looking at the rule again, we have the following:

–

For each
z_{i}=\texttt{{drop}}~x_{i}\in\overline{z=\mathit{use}}
, we remove the
reference

\mathit{ref}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathtt{root}(r),x_{i})=(\mathtt{root}(r)\xrightarrow{x_{i},k_{i}}\mathit{loc}_{x_{i}})

and add the reference

\mathtt{root}(r^{\prime})\xrightarrow{z_{i},k}\mathit{loc}_{x_{i}}

We also note that well-formedness of the effect and configuration,

\mathit{ref}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathtt{root}(r),x_{i})
is well defined, and
k_{i}=\textsf{iso}
.

For such an
i
, we let

\delta_{REF}^{i}=\begin{cases}(\{\mathtt{root}(r)\xrightarrow{x_{i},k_{i}}\mathit{loc}_{x_{i}}\},\{\mathtt{root}(r)\xrightarrow{z_{i},k_{i}}\mathit{loc}_{x_{i}}\})&\text{ if }k_{i}=\textsf{iso}\\
(\{\mathtt{root}(r)\xrightarrow{x_{i},k_{i}}\mathit{loc}_{x_{i}}\},\{\mathtt{root}(r)\xrightarrow{z_{i},\textsf{paused}{}\mathop{\odot}k_{i}}\mathit{loc}_{x_{i}}\})&\text{ otherwise}\end{cases}

–

For each
z_{i}=x_{i}\in\overline{z=\mathit{use}}
, if

\mathit{ref}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathtt{root}(r),x_{i})=(\mathtt{root}(r)\xrightarrow{x_{i},k_{i}}\mathit{loc}_{x_{i}}),

we add the reference

\mathtt{root}(r^{\prime})\xrightarrow{z_{i},\textsf{paused}\mathop{\odot}k_{i}}\mathit{loc}_{x_{i}}.

Well-formedness implies that
\mathit{ref}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathtt{root}(r),x_{i})
and

\textsf{paused}\mathop{\odot}k_{i}
is well defined.

For such an
i
, we let

\delta_{REF}^{i}=(\emptyset,\{\mathtt{root}(r)\xrightarrow{z_{i},k_{i}}\mathit{loc}_{x_{i}}\}).

–

We finally add a reference from
\mathtt{root}(r^{\prime})
to
\mathtt{temp}(r^{\prime},\iota^{\prime\prime})

\mathtt{root}(r^{\prime})\xrightarrow{w,k}\mathtt{temp}(r^{\prime},\iota^{\prime\prime})

(note that b.c. of well-formedness,
k\in\{\textsf{var},\mathtt{temp}\}
)
and from
\mathtt{temp}(r^{\prime},\iota^{\prime\prime})
to
\mathit{loc}_{f}
:

\mathtt{temp}(r^{\prime},\iota^{\prime\prime})\xrightarrow{\texttt{val},\textsf{mut}}\mathit{loc}_{f}

where

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathit{ref}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathtt{root}(r),y)&=&(\mathtt{root}(r)\xrightarrow{y,k_{y}}\mathit{loc}_{y})\\
\mathit{ref}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathit{loc}_{y},f)&=&(\mathit{loc}_{y}\xrightarrow{f,\textsf{iso}}\mathit{loc}_{f}).\end{array}

These are well defined because of well-formedness.

We let

\delta_{REF}^{\mathsf{entry}}=\left(\emptyset,\left\{(\mathtt{root}(r^{\prime})\xrightarrow{w,k}\mathtt{temp}(r^{\prime},\iota^{\prime\prime})),(\mathtt{temp}(r^{\prime},\iota^{\prime\prime})\xrightarrow{\texttt{val},\textsf{mut}}\mathit{loc}_{f})\right\}\right)

From the above we conclude that

\delta_{REF}=\sum_{i=1}^{\mathit{len}(\overline{z=\mathit{use}})}{\delta_{REF}^{i}}+\delta_{REF}^{\mathsf{entry}}

Case 2

\textit{badenter}(
):

The rule
REGION-ENTER-FAIL
has
\mathit{rcfg}=\mathit{rcfg}^{\prime}
and we conclude that

\delta^{*}(\rho,\mathcal{G},\textit{badenter}())=(\varepsilon,\varepsilon)

i.e.
\delta_{LOC}=\varepsilon
and
\delta_{REF}=\varepsilon
.

Case 3

\textit{exit}(x,\mathit{use},y.f,z.f^{\prime})
:

\delta^{*}(r::\rho,\mathcal{G},\textit{exit}(x,\mathit{use},y.f,z.f^{\prime}))=(\delta_{LOC},\delta_{REF})

We must now consider two cases:
REGION-EXIT-TEMP
and

REGION-EXIT-HEAP
. By examining both rules, we see that the top region
frame is popped. We can thus immediately conclude that
\mathtt{root}(r^{\prime})
and

\mathtt{temp}
-locations pertaining to the top region
r^{\prime}
are removed, i.e.

\displaystyle\delta_{LOC}=(

\displaystyle\{\mathtt{root}(r^{\prime})\}\uplus

\displaystyle\{\mathtt{temp}(r^{\prime},\_)~|~\mathtt{temp}(r^{\prime},\_)\in\mathcal{G}\},\emptyset)

For references, since we remove the top region frame all outgoing references
are removed. This corresponds to:

\displaystyle\delta_{REF}^{1}=(

\displaystyle\left\{\mathtt{root}(r^{\prime})\xrightarrow{\_,\_}\_~|~\mathtt{root}(r^{\prime})\xrightarrow{\_,\_}\_\in\mathcal{G}\right\}\uplus

\displaystyle\left\{\mathtt{temp}(r^{\prime},\_)\xrightarrow{\_,\_}\_~|~\mathtt{temp}(r^{\prime},\_)\xrightarrow{\_,\_}\_\in\mathcal{G}\right\},\emptyset)

The return value specified by
\mathit{use}
is picked up from the frame being
popped. Assuming
\mathit{use}=w
or
\mathit{use}=\texttt{{drop}}~w
, this means that we add
a reference:

\delta_{REF}^{2}=(\emptyset,\{\mathtt{root}(r)\xrightarrow{x,k_{w}}\mathit{loc}_{w}\})

where

\mathit{ref}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathtt{root}(r^{\prime}),w)=(\mathtt{root}(r^{\prime})\xrightarrow{w,k_{w}}\mathit{loc}_{w})

Note that
\mathit{ref}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathtt{root}(r^{\prime}),z)
is removed as part of
\delta_{REF}^{1}
above.

Lastly, we move the new entrypoint referred to by
z.f^{\prime}
in the top frame,
into
y.f
referred to in the second top frame:

\delta_{REF}^{3}=(\{\mathit{loc}_{y}\xrightarrow{f,k}\mathit{loc}_{f}\},\{\mathit{loc}_{y}\xrightarrow{f,k}\mathit{loc}_{f^{\prime}}\})

where

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathit{ref}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathtt{root}(r),y)&=&(\mathtt{root}(r)\xrightarrow{y,k_{y}}\mathit{loc}_{y})\\
\mathit{ref}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathit{loc}_{y},f)&=&(\mathit{loc}_{y}\xrightarrow{f,k}\mathit{loc}_{f})\\
\mathit{ref}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathtt{root}(r^{\prime}),z)&=&(\mathtt{root}(r^{\prime})\xrightarrow{z,k_{z}}\mathit{loc}_{f^{\prime}})\end{array}

In total we get:

\delta_{REF}=\delta_{REF}^{1}+\delta_{REF}^{2}+\delta_{REF}^{3}

Note that all of this holds for both
REGION-EXIT-TEMP
and

REGION-EXIT-HEAP
. The only difference between the two cases is the
format of
\mathit{loc}_{y}
.

Case 4

\textit{load}(x,y.f)
:

\delta^{*}(r::\rho,\mathcal{G},\textit{load}(x,y.f))=(\delta_{LOC},\delta_{REF})

It is clear from
REGION-LOAD
that

\delta_{LOC}=\varepsilon.

Furthermore, we see that the only action on references is adding a
reference from
\mathtt{root}(r)
to
\mathit{loc}_{f}
, the location referred to by
y.f
:

\delta_{REF}=(\emptyset,\{\mathtt{root}(r)\xrightarrow{x,k\mathop{\odot}k^{\prime}}\mathit{loc}_{f}\})

where

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathit{ref}(\mathtt{root}(r),y)&=&(\mathtt{root}(r)\xrightarrow{y,k}\mathit{loc}_{y})\\
\mathit{ref}(\mathit{loc}_{y},f)&=&(\mathit{loc}_{y}\xrightarrow{f,k^{\prime}}\mathit{loc}_{f})\end{array}

Case 5

\textit{swap}(x,y.f,\mathit{use})
:

The two rules for swap are
REGION-SWAP-TEMP
and
REGION-SWAP-HEAP
,
which are very similar. We note that
\textit{swap}()
has no action on the
locations of the graph:

\delta^{*}(r::\rho,\mathcal{G},\textit{swap}(x,y.f,\mathit{use}))=(\varepsilon,\delta_{REF})

We have
\mathit{use}=z
or
\mathit{use}=\texttt{{drop}}~z
. If we let

\mathit{ref}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathtt{root}(r),z)=(\mathtt{root}(r)\xrightarrow{z,k_{z}}\mathit{loc}_{z})

we have two cases:

\delta_{REF}^{\mathsf{drop}}=\begin{cases}(\{\mathtt{root}(r)\xrightarrow{z,k_{z}}\mathit{loc}_{z}\},\emptyset)&\text{ if }\mathit{use}=\texttt{{drop}}~z\\
\varepsilon&\text{ otherwise}\end{cases}

The action of the actual swap on the graph is

\delta_{REF}^{\mathsf{switch}}=(\{\mathit{loc}_{y}\xrightarrow{f,k_{y}}\mathit{loc}_{f}\},\{\mathit{loc}_{y}\xrightarrow{f,k_{z}}\mathit{loc}_{z}\})

In total we have

\delta_{REF}=\delta_{REF}^{\mathsf{drop}}+\delta_{REF}^{\mathsf{switch}}.

Because of the similarity, this holds in both
REGION-SWAP-TMP
and

REGION-SWAP-HEAP
. The only difference will be the format of
\mathit{loc}_{y}
.

Case 6

\textit{halloc}(x,k,\#C,\overline{\mathit{use}})
:

\delta^{*}(r::\rho,\mathcal{G},\textit{halloc}(x,\#C,k,\overline{\mathit{use}}))=(\delta_{LOC},\delta_{REF}).

By well-formedness,
k=\textsf{mut}
or
k=\textsf{iso}
, which corresponds to the two
cases
REGION-HALLOC-MUT
and
REGION-HALLOC-ISO
respectively. In both cases
the set of locations will be expanded with a new heap location with fresh
object id
\iota
. Its region id will depend on
k
:

r^{*}=\begin{cases}r&\text{ if }k=\textsf{mut}\\
r^{\prime}&\text{ if }k=\textsf{iso},\text{ where }r^{\prime}\text{ is fresh}\end{cases}

\delta_{LOC}=(\emptyset,\{\mathtt{heap}(r^{*},\iota)\})

Similar to the enter case (

Case 1

) above, we have a sequence of
\mathit{use}

to consider. These correspond to the fields of the new object being created.
Letting
\mathbf{fnames}(C)=f_{1},\dots,f_{n}
:

–

For each
\texttt{{drop}}~z_{i}\in\overline{\mathit{use}}
,

\mathit{ref}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathtt{root}(r),z_{i})=(\mathtt{root}(r)\xrightarrow{z_{i},k_{i}}\mathit{loc}_{i})

and

\delta_{REF}^{i}=(\{\mathtt{root}(r)\xrightarrow{z_{i},k_{i}}\mathit{loc}_{i}\},\{\mathtt{heap}(r^{*},\iota)\xrightarrow{f_{i},k_{i}}\mathit{loc}_{i}\})

–

For each
z_{i}\in\overline{\mathit{use}}
,

\mathit{ref}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathtt{root}(r),z_{i})=(\mathtt{root}(r)\xrightarrow{z_{i},k_{i}}\mathit{loc}_{i})

and

\delta_{REF}^{i}=(\emptyset,\{\mathtt{heap}(r^{*},\iota)\xrightarrow{f_{i},k_{i}}\mathit{loc}_{i}\})

This is all well defined because of well-formedness. In total we have

\delta_{REF}=(\emptyset,\{\mathtt{root}(r)\xrightarrow{x,k}\mathtt{heap}(r^{*},\iota)\})+\sum_{i=1}^{\mathit{len}(\overline{\mathit{use}})}\delta_{REF}^{i}

Case 7

\textit{salloc}(x,k,\mathsf{\#}\mathit{CL},\overline{\mathit{use}})
:

\delta^{*}(r::\rho,\mathcal{G},\textit{salloc}(x,k,C,\overline{\mathit{use}}))=(\delta_{LOC},\delta_{REF}).

By similar reasoning to

Case 6

, for a fresh
\iota

\delta_{LOC}=(\emptyset,\{\mathtt{temp}(r,\iota)\})

Letting
\mathbf{fnames}(C)=f_{1},\dots,f_{n}
:

–

For each
\texttt{{drop}}~z_{i}\in\overline{\mathit{use}}
,

\mathit{ref}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathtt{root}(r),z_{i})=(\mathtt{root}(r)\xrightarrow{z_{i},k_{i}}\mathit{loc}_{i})

and

\delta_{REF}^{i}=(\{\mathtt{root}(r)\xrightarrow{z_{i},k_{i}}\mathit{loc}_{i}\},\{\mathtt{temp}(r,\iota)\xrightarrow{f_{i},k_{i}}\mathit{loc}_{i}\})

–

For each
z_{i}\in\overline{\mathit{use}}
,

\mathit{ref}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathtt{root}(r),z_{i})=(\mathtt{root}(r)\xrightarrow{z_{i},k_{i}}\mathit{loc}_{i})

and

\delta_{REF}^{i}=(\emptyset,\{\mathtt{temp}(r,\iota)\xrightarrow{f_{i},k_{i}}\mathit{loc}_{i}\})

In total:

\delta_{REF}=(\emptyset,\{\mathtt{root}(r)\xrightarrow{x,k}\mathtt{temp}(r,\iota)\})+\sum_{i=1}^{\mathit{len}(\overline{\mathit{use}})}\delta_{REF}^{i}

We note that
k\in\{\textsf{tmp},\textsf{var}\}
by well-formedness.

Case 8

\textit{freeze}(x,\mathit{use})
:

By inspecting
REGION-FREEZE
,

\delta^{*}(r::rn,\mathcal{G},\textit{freeze}(x,\mathit{use}))=(\varepsilon,\delta_{REF})

i.e.
\delta_{LOC}=\varepsilon
.

By well-formedness,
\mathit{use}=\texttt{{drop}}~z
and

\mathit{ref}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathtt{root}(r),z)=(\mathtt{root}\xrightarrow{z,\textsf{iso}}\mathit{loc}_{z})

From rule
REGION-FREEZE
we have that this reference is consumed and
converted into a immutable reference:

\delta_{REF}=(\{\mathtt{root}(r)\xrightarrow{z,\textsf{iso}}\mathit{loc}_{z}\},\{\mathtt{root}(r)\xrightarrow{x,\textsf{imm}}\mathit{loc}_{z}\})

Case 9

\textit{merge}(x,\mathit{use})
:

\delta^{*}(r::rn,\mathcal{G},\textit{merge}(x,\mathit{use}))=(\delta_{LOC},\delta_{REF})

By well-formedness,
\mathit{use}=\texttt{{drop}}~z
. By
REGION-MERGE
,

\mathit{ref}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathtt{root}(r),z)=(\mathtt{root}(r)\xrightarrow{z,\textsf{iso}}\mathtt{heap}(r^{\prime},\iota^{\prime}))

The
\delta
s for merging a region is somewhat tricky. In the configuration
semantics, this ammounts to merging the subheap of the region
r^{\prime}
into the the
subheap of the currently active region
r
. This means that all locations

loc[r^{\prime}]
will be converted to
loc[r]
.

D_{LOC}=\left\{\mathit{loc}[r^{\prime}]~\middle|~\mathit{loc}[r^{\prime}]\in\mathcal{G}\right\}

We write

\delta_{LOC}=(D_{LOC},D_{LOC}[r^{\prime}/r])

where
D_{LOC}[r^{\prime}/r]
is the set of elements of
D_{LOC}
but with
r^{\prime}

substituted for
r
.

For references we have the analogue

D_{REF}=\left\{(\mathit{loc}_{1}[r_{1}]\xrightarrow{f,k}\mathit{loc}_{2}[r_{2}])~\middle|~(\mathit{loc}_{1}[r_{1}]\xrightarrow{f,k}\mathit{loc}_{2}[r_{2}])\in\mathcal{G}~\land~(r_{1}=r^{\prime}~\lor~r_{2}=r^{\prime})\right\}

We have

\delta_{REF}=(D_{REF},D_{REF}[r^{\prime}/r])

Case 10

\textit{cast}(x,\mathit{use},k~CL)
:

\mathit{use}
can either be
z
or
\texttt{{drop}}~z
. From the rule
REGION-CAST
it is
obvious that

\delta^{*}(r::\rho,\mathcal{G},\textit{cast}(x,\mathit{use},k~CL))=(\varepsilon,\delta_{REF})

where

\delta_{REF}=\delta_{REF}^{\mathsf{drop}}+(\emptyset,\{\mathtt{root}(r)\xrightarrow{x,k_{z}}\mathit{loc}_{z}\}

where

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathit{ref}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathtt{root}(r),z)&=&(\mathtt{root}(r)\xrightarrow{z,k_{z}}\mathit{loc}_{z})\\
\delta_{REF}^{\mathsf{drop}}&=&\begin{cases}(\{\mathtt{root}(r)\xrightarrow{z,k_{z}}\mathit{loc}_{z}\},\emptyset)&\text{ if }\mathit{use}=\texttt{{drop}}~z\\
\varepsilon&\text{ otherwise}\end{cases}\end{array}

Case 11

\textit{nocast}(x,\mathit{use},k~CL)
:

By similar reasoning to

Case 10

, using
REGION-NOCAST
instead:

\delta^{*}(r::\rho,\mathcal{G},\textit{nocast}(x,\mathit{use},k~CL))=(\varepsilon,\delta_{REF})

where

\delta_{REF}=\delta_{REF}^{\mathsf{drop}}+(\emptyset,\{\mathtt{root}(r)\xrightarrow{x,k_{z}}\mathit{loc}_{z}\}

where

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathit{ref}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathtt{root}(r),z)&=&(\mathtt{root}(r)\xrightarrow{z,k_{z}}\mathit{loc}_{z})\\
\delta_{REF}^{\mathsf{drop}}&=&\begin{cases}(\{\mathtt{root}(r)\xrightarrow{z,k_{z}}\mathit{loc}_{z}\},\emptyset)&\text{ if }\mathit{use}=\texttt{{drop}}~z\\
\varepsilon&\text{ otherwise}\end{cases}\end{array}

Case 12

\textit{bind}(\overline{x=\mathit{use}})
:

Looking at rule
REGION-BIND
, similarly to
\textit{halloc}()
and
\textit{salloc}()

cases (

Case 6

and

Case 7

), we have a sequence of

\mathit{use}
s. Here we instead create references from
\mathtt{root}(r)
. We get

\delta^{*}(r::\rho,\mathcal{G},\textit{bind}(\overline{x=\mathit{use}}))=(\varepsilon,\delta_{REF}).

–

For each
x_{i}=\texttt{{drop}}~z_{i}\in\overline{x=\mathit{use}}
,

\mathit{ref}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathtt{root}(r),z_{i})=(\mathtt{root}(r)\xrightarrow{z_{i},k_{i}}\mathit{loc}_{i})

and

\delta_{REF}^{i}=(\{\mathtt{root}(r)\xrightarrow{z_{i},k_{i}}\mathit{loc}_{i}\},\{\mathtt{root}(r)\xrightarrow{x_{i},k_{i}}\mathit{loc}_{i}\})

–

For each
x_{i}=z_{i}\in\overline{x=\mathit{use}}
,

\mathit{ref}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathtt{root}(r),z_{i})=(\mathtt{root}(r)\xrightarrow{z_{i},k_{i}}\mathit{loc}_{i})

and

\delta_{REF}^{i}=(\emptyset,\{\mathtt{root}(r)\xrightarrow{x_{i},k_{i}}\mathit{loc}_{i}\})

In total:

\delta_{REF}=\sum_{i=1}^{\mathit{len}(\overline{\mathit{use}})}\delta_{REF}^{i}
